# Changelog

Notable changes to Eregion. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and versions follow [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) — before 1.0, signals, schemas and interfaces may still change. Milestones and lots: [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md) (French).

## [Unreleased]

### Security
- **The reader of the red's mounts refuses what it cannot read** (SEC20b, #494, found by SEC20's second review; `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, « Ce que le rouge monte en écriture »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2). `tests/unit/test_red_mounts.py` read the current Makefile faithfully, but some forms make, docker or compose accept escaped it; none is in the Makefile today.
  - **Every launch, every mount**: launches are read as `docker run`, `docker create`, either as `docker container …`, whatever the spaces. Each launch's options are read as docker reads them, up to its image: a mount glued to `-v`, after `-v=`, in a group of short options (`-itv …`), by `--volume`, `--volume=` or `--mount`. `--volumes-from`, an option the reader does not know, a `--mount` key it does not read, and a launch behind docker's own options are refused. A recipe's continuation is read as the shell reads it, the two lines joined with nothing between; a comment continued onto the next line is refused, since the shell runs what follows.
  - **What a red launch is never given**, refused by name, never in the table of options read: `--privileged`, `--device`, `--cap-add`, `--pid`, `--userns`, `--ipc` or `--network` on the host (or another container), `--uts host`, a `--security-opt` other than `no-new-privileges:true`.
  - **No reference left unexpanded** in a launch's options, `$(HOME)`, `$(PWD)` and `$$HOME` aside: a variable of a `define` or of an included file, `$(shell …)`, a variable of the shell. And no `include`, `-include`, `sinclude`, `define`, `undefine`, `$(eval …)`, `SHELL`, `.SHELLFLAGS`, `.ONESHELL` or `load` in the Makefile: refused rather than read, since reading them would make the test a second make, and the Makefile needs none; nor a recipe's `$(MAKE)` told to read another makefile or directory (`-f`, `-C`).
  - **Compose reads its one file**: a recipe's compose launch given another file (`-f`, `--file`, `--project-directory`, `COMPOSE_FILE`) is refused, and so is any file beside `docker-compose.yml` that compose would take in its place or merge on its own (`compose.yaml`, `compose.yml`, `docker-compose.yaml`, their overrides, `docker-compose.override.yml` among them). The compose file includes no other (`include`), no service extends another or takes its mounts (`extends`, `volumes_from`), every image is written plainly.
  - **The shell's history with the fallback root image** (`UID=0`): appended, never truncated, as with the operator's image. The neighbour file bash truncates through is made in the container's own layer, and its rename onto the mounted file fails (`EBUSY`, a rule of the kernel, not measured here: no container); bash then removes the neighbour (read in its code, not measured); nothing reaches the host. Measured without a container (bash 5.2): a directory that refuses the neighbour leaves the history appended, never truncated. A session longer than `HISTSIZE` is not kept, with either image (the Makefile's comment and `docs/fichiers-cles.md` say it).
  - Mutants M3490–M3497, M3525–M3529 and M3535–M3536 (each form, which SEC20's reader missed, `--volumes-from` aside) and M3498–M3499 (`ANNATAR_SHELL_FILES`: the link check removed, the files made before it). The shell probe runs with its own `HOME` and `HISTFILE`.

### Fixed
- **A perimeter pair another VM wrote again, gone again before any pass, is said for each VM** (L1W, #492; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1W »; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). Found by L1E's rule and frozen in strict `xfail`: web of rg-app2 blocked on its subnet, the pair taken off, said in human_only, the card acknowledged; web of rg-app1 blocked on the same subnet wrote the pair again (one per IP and subnet), taken off again before any pass. Azure read as the card had read, and rg-app2/web's entry stayed silent behind its acknowledged card — only rg-app1/web's card said it. **Glorfindel now notes its own writes of a perimeter pair**, before each write (`~/.glorfindel/perimeter_writes.jsonl`, `glorfindel/response/pair_journal.py`, appended, 0600): an acknowledged card that said the pair gone (absent, or there and turned) is over once that pair is noted after its acknowledgement (`reassert_said._written_since`) — what is still gone is said anew, or put back once outside human_only. A pair held at the card, a write before the acknowledgement, a pending card: nothing more. A write that reached Azure and then raised is noted too (a card more, never one less); a journal refused never stops the block, it is logged. The journal is read line by line, a byte out of UTF-8 replaced (one forged line no longer silences it), and opened only without following a link and only as a regular file (`O_NOFOLLOW`, `fstat`): a link or a pipe in its place is refused, its target untouched (M3530–M3534). And `_back` reads a turned pair back only if it reads otherwise now (`was != is_`, L1H's review). The oracle and the machine unchanged (A0). Tests: the `xfail` lifted, the oracle's control (`test_a_perimeter_written_again_then_gone_with_its_journal_lost_is_refused`, which keeps M3442 caught), `test_reassert.py` (acknowledged or pending, in human_only or not, said once and not in a loop, the first time, the journal, the adapter). Mutants M3480–M3489.
- **A control run before Orodruin's tag: the layers present and unpacked** (OI2, #490, found by OI1's review; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « L'image des injecteurs »). Under containerd's image store, `docker image inspect` reads the config: `RootFS.Layers` comes from it, and `docker tag` reads nothing else. A layer lost after a load that succeeded passed the read-back and the tag, and showed only at the image's first run. `make orodruin-push` now starts the image read back as the proven one once before tagging it `:orodruin`: `timeout -k 10 120 docker run --rm` hardened as every injector's run (`ORO_HARDEN`: read-only root, `/tmp` in tmpfs, no capability, no privilege gained), `--pull never --network none --no-healthcheck --name eregion-annatar-check --entrypoint true eregion-annatar:incoming` — no mount, no `orodruin.env`, no environment. Creating the container needs every layer. If it fails or passes its bound (120 s; `ORO_CHECK_TIMEOUT=<s>` on the command line, digits, at least 1, no leading zero — `timeout 00` sets no bound —, checked before anything is sent and never expanded by make, like `REF`), its container (`timeout 30 docker rm -f`) and `incoming` are removed, the cause is said (docker's words, then the bound, docker unable to create the container, `true` that does not run, ssh, or the code), `:orodruin` is unchanged and `~/.eregion-image` stays absent, as under OI1. A check container left by a push cut short is removed at the start of the next one, with the `incoming` left. An image not read back as the proven one is never run, not even for this check.
  - **Within the contract**: no network (only loopback, so no IMDS and no token for the managed identity), never pulled, nothing injected (`true` replaces the image's command: no Python, no injector), no cloud call to journal, nothing mounted, bounded and removed. It runs in a narrower surface than the first real run, which follows the tag. The six guarantees are taken one by one in the contract.
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_published_image.py` on OI1's fake docker (`tests/unit/fake_oci.py` now creates containers as docker does: the config read and every layer unpacked, else exit 125; `FAKE_LOAD_DROP=layer`, `FAKE_RUN_HANG`, `FAKE_RUN_EXIT`, a container left holds its image). The case of a layer lost after the load is red on main, under both stores (the image tagged `:orodruin`, then the migration fails). Mutants M3470–M3479, M3520–M3524. To measure, with Jonathan: `make orodruin-push REF=<the commit of main>`, then `make orodruin-check`.
- **« Reposé une fois » ends its episode: a block gone again before the next pass is said « une deuxième fois »** (L1O, #507, found by L1W's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1O »; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »). On `reapplied`, `_rewrite_block` (`glorfindel/reassert.py`) wrote `reasserted_at` but kept the drift marks of the episode before (`drift_alerted_at`, `drift_said`) and left that episode open in memory. A block put back once could then go again before the next pass, in the state the put-back had read. It read as « already said, no worse » and the pass stayed silent, though the card « reposé une fois », pending or acknowledged, says « s'il disparaît encore, Glorfindel alertera sans le reposer ». One way there is L1G's: a failed put-back is acknowledged, then a rule of the pair is written again. Now the episode ends in the write of `reasserted_at`. It is closed in memory first, whatever that write does, and the marks are taken off in the same write. The next disappearance is said « une deuxième fois » and never put back (L1Q), and nothing is written in `human_only`. A put-back that does not hold whole (`partial`) still marks a new episode after its card. The first time (`reassert_perimeters.py`, L1Qs) ends the episode the same way in its own write of `reasserted_at`: a pass whose second time skipped the entry (its lock held) left the marks and the episode open, and the pair gone again behind the acknowledged card stayed silent.
  - **Where it showed**: in the reassertion's own logic, with a connector that does not rewrite the block's entry. On Azure, the adapter's state write (`_save_block_state`) takes the drift marks off at every whole block (L1Q), so the episode already ended on disk. The invariant machine, which drives the real adapter, cannot produce the defect, and both ways replayed through it are green on main. The core no longer depends on that side effect (MC0).
  - Tests: `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`, `test_l1o_*`. They cover L1G's way for the VM's own block and for a merged entry, with the card pending or acknowledged, in both modes; the marks on disk; no loop; the write refused; a partial put-back still marked; a restart then a refused write (the closure in memory alone); and a pass cut between its two times. 16 of the 18 are red on main; the two green ones are guards. L1W's way, added after L1W's merge: a pair put back once, written again by another perimeter (noted in `perimeter_writes.jsonl`) and gone before the next pass is said once for each VM (`test_l1o_the_way_of_l1w_*`, red on main for the card pending or acknowledged after the write; `test_a_pair_put_back_once_written_again_by_another_perimeter_then_gone_is_said_for_each_vm_once`). `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_a_block_put_back_once_acknowledged_then_gone_again_is_said_a_second_time` checks the card's promise end to end and is green on main. Mutants M3540–M3548.
- **The canary's OIDC subject is the immutable one GitHub issues** (REN1, #470; `docs/operations.md`, « Le canari azurerm, sans secret »). Since the account's rename, GitHub issues for this repository the subject `repo:<owner>@<owner id>/<repository>@<repository id>:…` (`use_immutable_subject`, read on `repos/…/actions/oidc/customization/sub`): the subject by name that REN1's entry gave (`[0.5.3]`) never matched — every line of the canary failed at its login, `AADSTS700213` (measured on 04/11). The procedure now reads the prefix from the API, so no id enters the repository; the federated credential trusts the immutable subject, and the two by name are removed (Jonathan, 04/11). Better than before: the subject no longer rests on a name someone else could take — an id is never given again. The name stays in it, so a later rename will likely change it again.
  - Tests: `test_workflows.py` — the subject read from the API, the heredoc left to expand it, no id in the section.

## [0.5.3] — 2026-11-04 — "signed and sealed"

### Security
- **The watch's heartbeat signed, the harness's attestation signed by its author** (SEC19, #456; N-03 and R-13 of the second counter-audit, now held; `docs/design/watch-key.md`; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`; `docs/operations.md`, « Le battement signé, l'attestation signée — les gestes »; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « SEC19 change l'agent » and « La signature de l'attestation »).
  - **The watch's key** (`eregion_core/watch_key.py`): an Ed25519 pair drawn at the watch's first start in `~/.eregion/watch-key/` (0600 in 0700), a directory only the watch's service mounts — never `~/.glorfindel` (the console, the bot, the injectors mount it), never `runs/`. A key readable by others, a link, another owner, another size, a misplaced directory: never used, said; the heartbeats then go unsigned, and the injectors refuse them. The directory is made as the operator before the services start (`make glorfindel-start`/`-restart`/`-dev`, `team-start`, H0's `deploy.sh`). `glorfindel watch-key`, `make watch-key`, `make glorfindel-host-watch-key` give its public half (`EREGION_WATCH_KEY=ed25519:…`) and its fingerprint, never the private one.
  - **The heartbeat signed**: the injectors' copy and the return channel's `heartbeat.json` carry `seq` (a counter that only rises), `key` (the signer's fingerprint, said, never trusted) and `sig`, over the domain `eregion.watch-beat/1` and the canonical JSON of every other field (the channel's `since` and `windows` too). The injectors (`eregion_core/watch_gate.py`, D1) verify it with the public key pinned on their side, `EREGION_WATCH_KEY` — orodruin.env, the operator's environment, passed by compose and the Makefile —, never read in the channel nor in the heartbeat. Refused before any call to the cloud, the reason said: no key pinned (the gesture named), unsigned, signed by another key, a signature that does not hold (a field changed, a copied fingerprint, a signature for another use), a counter below one already seen (`runs/orodruin/.watch_seen.json`). The console shows the gate without keeping that counter. `--without-glorfindel` stays the development waiver, written in the report. The local channel of verdicts (SEC18) stays unsigned: the gate never reads it. FS1 (#413) takes this key up under its own domain (`eregion.action-queue/1`).
  - **The attestation signed by its author's key** (R-13; Jonathan's decision, 03/11: a dedicated cosign key, no public log — no address published, in the repository nor at sigstore): `make harness-key`, once, has cosign draw the pair in `~/.config/eregion/harness/` (off the repository; the private half encrypted by the passphrase Jonathan types at a terminal — never `COSIGN_PASSWORD`, filtered out for the key and the signature; empty, the key is unprotected —, 0600; no terminal, a destination under the repository, a link or a key already there refused; `*.key` in `.gitignore`) and prints `EREGION_HARNESS_KEY=…` — the public key's DER in base64, one line, to pin in `host.env`. `make harness-sign`, after `make llm-compare … ATTEST=1`, signs `requirements/harness.json` offline (`cosign sign-blob --key --signing-config <no service>`: measured with cosign v3.1.3, the network refused — it has no `--tlog-upload`, and `--use-signing-config=false` alone still posts to Rekor), the bundle `requirements/harness.json.sigstore.json` beside it holding the signature and the key's hint only (refused and removed if it held a certificate or a log entry); `make harness-verify` says what H0 will say. H0's CD verifies it by `cosign verify-blob --key <host.env's key> --insecure-ignore-tlog`, its pinned cosign, the key written to a 0600 temporary file, before reading the attestation — never a key from the repository. Unsigned, another key, a signature of another file, no key declared on H0 or one unreadable (cosign never asked): refused and said with the mention, the lock's guard holding as without an attestation. Offline, nothing to wait for: every failure is a refusal.
  - **The transition**: the signature is asked at each change of `requirements/glorfindel.txt` once SEC19's agent is installed, and only then; no earlier attestation is ever read again (each is bound to its lock's sha256), so the first signed one is the next lock's. No grace by date (it is in the file) nor by commit. SEC19's own lock change leaves `requirements/glorfindel.txt` as it is: `cryptography` is declared in the `annatar` extra (the injectors verify; `make lock` adds a `# via` line to `annatar.txt` and `dev.txt`), the watch keeps it through azure-identity — declared in `glorfindel` with its next lock change, which brings the harness anyway.
  - **For Jonathan, in order — REN1 and SEC19 together, for the 0.5.3** (an agent installed by REN1's list alone, before SEC19's merge, is installed again): `make harness-key`, its line in `host.env`, `make glorfindel-host-push`; `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` **before publishing** (the agent's `deploy.sh` makes the watch key's directory; the one before would leave it to docker, as root); the release, `cd-check`, `cd-enable`; H0's watch key (`make glorfindel-host-watch-key`) in `host.env` and `orodruin.env`, pushed, then on H0 `docker compose up -d war-room` (the console reads its environment again; Dagorlad refuses every request until then); `make orodruin-push REF=<tag>`, `make orodruin-check`; the workstation's watch restarted, its key pinned in `.envrc`, restarted again; at the next lock, `make llm-compare … ATTEST=1`, `make harness-sign`, `make harness-verify`.
  - **What stays**: whoever reads the watch's private key (root on its host) signs as it; a captured heartbeat stays replayable within its 90 s until a newer one is read; the attestation's author is whoever holds its key — a stolen key signs as Jonathan, with no transparency log to see it (the sessions run as his Unix user: the key is encrypted by a typed passphrase, under `~/.config/eregion/`, which the sessions never read; a hardware key, `cosign --sk`, as an option); rotating it is a new pair pinned again, revoking it unpinning it.
  - The watch's key drawn whole in a file of its own, then published by a link and its directory fsync'd: a full disk never leaves an empty key under its name, and a temporary a crash left (a copy of the private key) is swept at the next start, never followed (the reviews). `make harness-sign` passes no `--yes`; `verify-blob` without a key says the gesture. Mutants M3425–M3429. `make glorfindel-shell` does not mount it: said by `glorfindel watch-key` there.
  - Tests: `test_watch_key.py` (a pair drawn in the test, a fixed seed), `test_watch_gate.py`, `test_gothmog.py` (refused before any cloud call), `test_host_cd.py` and `test_published_image.py` (a fake `cosign verify-blob --key`, `tests/unit/fake_sigstore.py`, answering as v3.1.3 did when measured); mutants M3240–M3259, M1230, M1236 and M1660 re-anchored.
- **The red never writes what the operator runs** (SEC20, #467, found by SEC18's review; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2; `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, « Ce que le rouge monte en écriture »). An injector's container (Annatar, Gothmog) mounts read-write no directory of code, scripts or configuration that the operator, the watch or a `make` runs afterwards, nor one that holds one (the repository whole, the home whole): only data — its directories of runs/ (SEC18), Orodruin's migration of `~/runs` once, and the history of Annatar's shell.
  - **Read-only now**: `scripts/` and `annatar/scenarios/`, in the compose service and in `ANNATAR_VOLS` (`make annatar-run`, `annatar-dry-run`, `annatar-shell`), and the scenarios of `make annatar-validate` and `annatar-list`. Annatar reads `scripts/vm/` and sends a script to its target by Run Command; it reads its scenarios; it writes neither. The mounts stay, so a local change serves without a rebuild. A script that writes, `setup_testdata.sh`, writes on its target VM (`/mnt/testdata`), never on the host.
  - **`~/.annatar` leaves the compose service**: Annatar's CLI writes nothing there, and its `.bashrc` is what `make annatar-shell` runs (`--init-file`). The shell mounts two files of it, never the directory: its `.bashrc` read-only, its history read-write, where `HISTFILE` now puts it (nothing set it since the `.bashrc` the Makefile writes holds only the alias: the history was not kept). Bash appends to that file; it no longer truncates it (its truncation goes through a file beside it, which the container cannot make). The recipe makes both first, as the operator, and refuses a link in place of `~/.annatar` or of either file, or anything but a file (`ANNATAR_SHELL_FILES`): Docker resolves a bind's source on the host. `make profile-detection` and `profile-proven` no longer make `~/.annatar`.
  - **Orodruin unchanged**: its docker runs (`ORO_RUN`, `ORO_RUN_GOTHMOG`, `ORO_RUN_CONTRACT`, `ORO_RUN_REQUEST`, `ORO_MIGRATE`) already mounted neither scripts nor code — no `make orodruin-push` needed for this lot.
  - Tests: `test_red_mounts.py` — reads the compose file and every `docker run` of the injectors' image in the Makefile (the workstation's and Orodruin's, every line expanded as make would, the last definition kept): no red container writes a directory of code, scripts or configuration, nor what holds one, nor the file a red shell runs at its start; each writes only its runs/ and the shell's history file; the inventory of what each writes. What such a reader would not see is refused: a variable of a red run defined twice or appended to (`+=`), a `docker run` whose image is not written plainly, a `docker compose … run` that adds a mount. Red on `main`. `test_profiles.py`: the compose service no longer writes `~/.annatar`. Mutants M3400–M3409.

### Changed
- **The repository follows its owner's account, `Vanyar53` → `grootcyber`** (REN1, #470): `grootcyber/eregion`, the Pages at `grootcyber.github.io/eregion`, the images at `ghcr.io/grootcyber/eregion-*`, `CODEOWNERS`, the publishing workflow's guard, the defaults of the scripts and of H0's CD, the schemas' `$id`, the docs. GitHub redirects the repository's web, git and API addresses; never the Pages, the profile, the OIDC subject, nor the images: they followed the account, and the old namespace answers 403 (measured on 03/11).
  - **The images' identity** (Jonathan's decision, 03/11): `scripts/published_image.py` and H0's CD verify `grootcyber/eregion`'s publishing workflow only. An image published before the rename (v0.5.2 and older) is signed by `Vanyar53/eregion`, a name anyone may now claim: a way back asked on H0 towards it is refused, said; the way out is `make glorfindel-host-start REF=<tag>`, which builds on the VM. The automatic way back after a failed deployment is untouched — it runs the image already on H0. The first release after the rename is the next way back.
  - **The canary's OIDC**: its subject becomes `repo:grootcyber/eregion:environment:canary` — a second federated credential before the rename, the old one removed after a green canary (`docs/operations.md`, « Le canari azurerm, sans secret »).
  - **Reviews**: `review_verdict.py --author grootcyber`, never the old login — a comment's author is the account, renamed everywhere, so a verdict published before the rename reads `grootcyber` (measured on 03/11); `Vanyar53`, free to anyone, would let a stranger's comment pass for the maintainer's.
  - **Kept on purpose**: H0's `eregion_repo` default (`infra/glorfindel-host/variables.tf`) — cloned once at first boot through `custom_data`, changing it replaces the VM; GitHub's redirect serves it until H0 is rebuilt. And the released entries below, true when written.
  - **For Jonathan, after the merge, in order** (`infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « REN1 change l'agent »): `make glorfindel-host-cd-install` from an up-to-date `main`; the 0.5.3 published; `make glorfindel-host-cd-check` (« ✅ À blanc : v0.5.3 est prête » — before the 0.5.3 it is red on the v0.5.2 in service, signed by the old name: expected); `make glorfindel-host-cd-enable`. Orodruin: `make orodruin-push REF=<the merge's sha>`, then `make orodruin-check`. A canary run, then the old federated credential removed.
  - Tests: `test_repo_owner.py` — the old login in no tracked file but history's (the changelog, the historical notes, the canary's note, H0's `eregion_repo` default, one line each where counted), and the new one where it is trusted: the images' owner and source, the publishing guard, the CD's default, CODEOWNERS, the reviewers.

### Fixed
- **A VM's blocks in the console: each button acts on its own IP, every block is said** (CON-K, #498; `docs/design/console.md`, « Les blocages d'une VM »). Expanded, a VM card attached the VM's first block to its decision row and hid the others whenever a block decision was under 24 h old; collapsed, one badge per IP made it as long as the list. « Extend to subnet » promoted the VM's first block whatever row it sat on, and said « the whole subnet » whatever the promotion left out; « Unblock » ran a reset — every block of the VM and its isolation.
  - **The card** (Jonathan's decision A, 03/11): several blocks hold in one line, collapsed and expanded (« BLOCKED ×N IPs · newest … · oldest … »); expanded, the latest decision keeps its row and its reasoning, and « Manage the N blocks ▸ » opens a window listing them all, newest first, filterable by IP or NSG, each with « Extend to subnet » and « Unblock ». A single block is shown with its buttons, no window.
  - **Each button names its IP**: `POST /api/action/block-promote/<vm>?ip=…` and a new `POST /api/action/unblock/<vm>?ip=…`. The IP is looked up among that VM's active blocks: none named, or one the VM does not block, is refused and nothing runs; two VMs of that name, refused. « Unblock » runs `glorfindel unblock` for that IP alone — the VM's other blocks and its isolation stay; a partial unblock (exit 2) is said as a failure, an IP of which no rule is found as « already removed? », never « unblocked ». The subnet-wide row of the PROTECT card too, its confirmation saying its reach: the rule goes from the subnet's NSG, every VM there reachable from the IP again. The command shown is the argv run; the window finds its VM by its resource id, never by its name.
  - **A promotion says what it leaves out**: a NIC on another subnet or unreadable (`blocked_partial`), a rule the promotion kept (I4P), an allow that passes before the deny, other ports still reachable — each named as the connector names it, escaped, in a window (« extended — what it leaves out »: other ports reachable leave the subnet covered). « Extended to the whole subnet » only when the outcome says the subnet rule holds whole (`whole`).
  - **The drills' advice** (DG-d's follow-up): for a name several enrolled VMs bear, `make orodruin-exercise DRILL=…` says « load it on the board and choose the VM there » — saved again, the drill would name the VM the same way.
  - Checked in Chromium on the console's sample (seven blocks on one VM, one on another): the card, the window, the filter, a row's Unblock and the request it makes, a partial promotion said; no JS error, no CSP violation. `python scripts/console_escaping.py`: no raw write. Tests: `test_console_blocks.py`; mutants M3387–M3389, M3460–M3469.
- **A perimeter pair the card read turned, written again by another VM's perimeter block, ends the acknowledged episode** (L1H, #483, found by L1A's measure, seed 5 at 500; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1H »). A merged entry — its perimeter pair and the VM's own rules —, the pair turned into an allow and the VM's rules deleted: the card says them gone, the pair too (« gone » by what it does), the operator acknowledges it. Then another VM blocked on the same subnet writes the pair again, a deny: the VM's own rules still gone, the pair whole, read « better than the card », in silence — the card had read the pair on Azure, and a rule « came back » only from absent (L1G). Now a rule of the entry's perimeter pair the card said gone while reading it there, holding now, was written again: the acknowledged episode is over, what is still gone is put back once or said (`reassert_said._back`; the first time of the pass decides the same). The pair only: a VM's own rules are written by its own block alone, and one turned then set right in place still says nothing (RB1); a turned pair set right by hand reads as written again — a card more rather than a disappearance unsaid. The oracle is unchanged (A0). Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_an_acknowledged_drift_whose_turned_pair_another_perimeter_wrote_again_is_said` (twenty rules as found, three reduced; red on `main` d597a00e), `test_reassert.py` (the turned pair written again, acknowledged or pending, outside `human_only`, the first time; nothing more when it stays turned, when a VM's own rule is set right, when a pair held changes); mutants M3430–M3436, M1943/M3280/M3281 re-anchored.
- **The image of Annatar arrives whole on Orodruin** (OI1, #488, found by measuring after REN1 on 03/11; `docs/design/orodruin-contract.md`, « L'image des injecteurs »). `make orodruin-push` sent the image through the operator's `docker save`; under Docker Desktop, with its containerd image store, `docker save` of a pulled image gives its index and its manifest without config or layers (~10 KB for ~630 MB), so Orodruin loaded an image its own `docker tag` could not read (« failed to read config content: NotFound »), and `published_image.py loaded`, which compared the id alone, passed it.
  - **The archive is built from the registry's blobs** (`published_image.py archive`): the linux/amd64 manifest under the digest cosign proved, then its config and its layers, read anonymously by their digest (a redirect to GHCR's storage never carries the registry's token); each blob checked by its sha256 and its size against the manifest before it enters; a size out of bounds (read in the manifest, before a byte is fetched), a missing blob, a wrong sha256, an unexpected media type (a foreign layer), a config of another commit, another platform or a root user: nothing is sent, the cause said. The archive (`oci-layout`, `index.json`, docker's `manifest.json`, `blobs/sha256/…`, as `docker save` writes it) goes over SSH, Orodruin's egress still closed, into `docker load`, under the waiting tag `eregion-annatar:incoming`. The operator's docker is no longer asked to pull or save.
  - **`loaded` reads the image back on Orodruin** (`docker image inspect`, its whole JSON): its id (the manifest's under containerd's store, the config's digest under the classic store), the layers and the user of its config, its platform, its commit — against what the proven manifest and its config announce. An image without its config reads back without layers nor user, or not at all (docker's CLI applies `-f` on its side: the `-f {{.Id}}` that answered came from a partial JSON of the same API call): refused, `incoming` removed, `eregion-annatar:orodruin` as it was, `~/.eregion-image` absent until success. `loaded --id` is gone: an id alone is no longer an answer. The manifests are read through the same opener as the blobs: no redirect carries the registry's token to another host. A root user is refused whatever its spelling (`0`, `00`, `000:0`, `root`). The archive's temporary directory is removed at a Ctrl-C too (`INT`, `TERM`, `HUP`: dash runs no `EXIT` trap on a signal).
  - **The limits**: that the containerd store of Docker Desktop throws away the compressed layers once unpacked is a hypothesis, not measured; OI1 does not depend on it. `docker image inspect` does not say whether the layers are unpacked: a run without network before the tag would prove it — OI2 (#490). To measure, with Jonathan: `make orodruin-push REF=<the commit of main>`, then `make orodruin-check`.
  - Tests: `test_published_image.py` — a fake registry serving blobs (and GHCR's redirect), a fake docker that reads an archive as both stores do (`tests/unit/fake_oci.py`, whose `save` of a pulled image drops config and layers, as measured); `make orodruin-push` run for real on them: the image arrives whole, red on `main` (the measured « failed to read config content »); a config lost (read back as its id alone), another id, another user refused; a Ctrl-C leaves no archive; a blob altered or missing stops everything before any ssh. Mutants M3450–M3459 (M1664 and M1699 follow the code).
- **A card is closed in the write of the card that replaces it, never before** (L1A, #480; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1A »). Seed 42 at 500, found by I5S's measure and shrunk to four rules of `main`: « L1: the block vanished from Azure; the reassertion neither put it back nor said so ». A block's drift said again (worse) closed the pending card (« Remplacée : une nouvelle alerte… »), then wrote the new one — refused (a full disk), no card was left, `drift_alerted_at` kept from before; the next pass, the block unverified (behind the quarantine, the configuration unreadable), said nothing. `escalations.record(replaces={id: note})` now closes the cards it replaces in the same write as the new one (a closure by the operator meanwhile kept): refused, the former card still waits, and the mark is not written. The same order for every card the reassertion replaces: its alerts (`reassert._alert`), a changed bypass, another set of left rules, another set of unreadable state files, a quarantine's changed diff, an orphan's card whose address passed (U1O) or said before the turn (I5Q). `say_unverified` replaced nothing. Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_a_block_said_again_as_the_disk_fills_keeps_a_card_through_an_unchecked_pass` (as found and shrunk, red on `main` 41bef33), seven in `test_reassert.py` and two in `test_orphan_sweep.py` (the store refusing the write that adds a card), red on `main`; mutants M3410–M3419; M833, M1257, M1861, M1862, M1932, M1934, M1939, M1940, M1946, M2265, M3101 re-anchored (M1861 named `_resolve`, absent from its module: a NameError; it and M1865 now read a closure's note in `test_a_quarantine_whole_again_then_back_is_said_while_its_record_is_kept`). Two other L1s, red on `main` too, of another cause, are left to follow-up lots (`docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1A »).
- **The invariant machine's I5 per VM knows a writer's perimeter carried as a NIC's NSG — and bounds what it admits** (I5S, #477, after I4P's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I5S »). A test-only change, no Glorfindel code touched. A VM in `human_only` whose NIC carries, as its own NSG, the subnet NSG a writer (`non_disruptive`) holds its perimeter on loses its flows to the attacker when the pass puts that perimeter back: Azure applies the same rule to the same NSG, on a subnet or on a NIC. The code was right; the oracle (`_shared_with_writers`, L1Qs) read « shared with a writer » as « behind its subnet » only, and said « I5: the reassertion acted on … in human_only ». **Widened by Jonathan's decision (03/11, A0), and bounded**: `perimeter_nic`, the NICs whose NIC's NSG is a writer's perimeter; a flow shut there is admitted only if it goes to the attacker and the perimeter rules the pass wrote **on a writer's perimeter NSG** — the NSG shared — close it on their own (`_closed_by_the_perimeter_alone`, I4P's computation: `world_before` plus the journal's writes under the IP's name alone; for I5, only those on the writers' perimeter NSGs read before the pass, `on` — the review: on any NSG, the IP's pair written on one no writer holds passed for a NIC carrying a writer's NSG, a refusal of before relaxed; I4 calls it unfiltered, as before). The controls of today stay (no rule written or taken off in the name of a NIC in `human_only`, its NICs' NSGs unchanged). **Hardened**: the subnet's branch, which admitted any attacker flow of a NIC behind it, now asks the same third condition. I4P's restriction lifted (`promoted_beside_a_turned_block` draws a subnet's NSG on a NIC of another subnet again); seed 7 at 500 frozen (`test_a_writers_perimeter_on_the_nic_of_a_vm_in_human_only_is_shared`, red on the narrow oracle), with the oracle's own controls (a pair aimed at `*` among them: the first condition is not implied by the third). Mutants M3383, M3384, M3386, M3390–M3392.
- **The promotion of a block to the perimeter never cuts off another VM** (I4P, #466, found by I4G's review; `docs/azure.md`, « La promotion en périmètre ne coupe jamais une autre VM » ; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I4P »). Promoted (`block_suspicious_ip(scope="subnet", replace=True)`), a VM's block took its per-NIC rules off by their names, without the guard every other removal by name asks (`_held_by_turned`, I4B): one someone turned into an allow, which let the attacker reach another VM — its address taken up since, or the NIC's NSG given to a VM of another subnet, out of the perimeter's reach — went, and that VM was cut off. Both rules of a pair now ask the guard first (`perimeter._promote`); one kept, the pair stays whole, on Azure and in the state for the unblock, said in `kept_rules` and the note as the unblock says it; the status stays the VM's (`blocked`, or `blocked_partial` for a NIC off the perimeter). A delete that fails stops the pair's, its error said: the first refused, the second is not tried and the pair stays whole; the second refused, the first is gone — either way the placement stays in the state and the unblock reads it again (before, swallowed: the rule left on Azure, out of the state). The advice of an orphan deny kept for a turned rule behind it (I5Q, `_turned_text`, already on `main`) said « la remettre en refus » of an isolation's rule too — put back, it would cut every flow of the VM at that address: said for a block's rule on its addresses only. The advice of a kept rule says « la remettre en refus » for a block's rule on one address only — an isolation's, put back, would cut every flow of the VM that holds the address. The invariant machine judged a perimeter block without the attacker's flows of the other VMs: it now cuts only what its perimeter rules close (`_cut_by_the_perimeter_alone`, I4 — nothing removed or narrowed), and a composite rule poses the world (`promoted_beside_a_turned_block`, on NICs' NSGs); three sequences frozen, red on `main`. Mutants M3350–M3359.
- **The invariant machine no longer forgets the acknowledgement of a VM's block when an isolation of that VM comes back** (MAK, #481, found by L1A in the CI of PR #476; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « MAK »). A defect of the test machine, not of Glorfindel: it failed the CI at random seeds with a false « L1: the block on …/web vanished from Azure; the reassertion neither put it back nor said so ». `said_then_back_after_a_full_disk`'s « gone » branch, once the rules it had taken off were back, dropped `acked_drift[vm]` whichever branch it ran — the operator's acknowledgement of the VM's block, which the isolation branch never touched; the block's rules still as acknowledged, the code rightly said nothing more, and L1 counted the block unsaid. It is now dropped by the block branch only. The oracle is unchanged (A0): an acknowledged block counts as said only while its rules are exactly as at the acknowledgement — values, not an episode: a block written again then gone again identically counts the acknowledgement again (an older limit of RB1/L1G's snapshot, not MAK's; tracked in #485); the machine's journal records only what the test did. Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_an_isolation_back_keeps_the_acknowledgement_of_the_vms_block` (the two CI runs as found, twelve rules, and reduced by hand to six; red on `main` de040e8c); `_replay` calls a rule without draws as Hypothesis does. The CI's three logs replayed: the two of this defect green, the third (L1P's) already green on `main`. The seed 5 at 500 found by L1A stays red: another cause, left to a follow-up lot. Mutants M3420–M3423.
- **An acknowledged drift card of a block no longer counts for a new episode** (L1E, #485, found by MAK's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1E »). A hole in the invariant machine's oracle, not in Glorfindel, and on `main` since RB1: L1 counted an acknowledged block as said while its rules were exactly as at the acknowledgement — values, never an episode. A block written again whole, then gone again the same way, found the values of the acknowledgement again, and the card of the episode before counted the new disappearance said: with the drift marks kept by a block written again (L1Q's mutant, `response/state.py`, `for mark in ():`), the code was silent and L1 passed. **A rule added to the machine's journal, nothing taken away** (A0): the acknowledgement stops counting once the test wrote the block whole again (`_block`, `block_perimeter`, « blocked »), or once the fake's journal shows a write of Glorfindel's, after it, on a rule the acknowledgement saw absent (a block put back, a pair another VM's perimeter wrote) — never the code's marks (`IsolationMachine._ack_holds`). Tests: `test_invariant_regressions.py::test_a_block_written_again_then_gone_as_acknowledged_is_a_new_episode` (MAK's review's probe as rules of the machine, on its network and a plain one; green on `main` d597a00e, red with the mutant). Mutants M3440–M3442. **Found by the rule, by hand, left open**: a perimeter pair another VM's block wrote again, then gone again before any pass, is said on that VM's card alone — the first VM, its card acknowledged, silent; frozen in strict `xfail` (`test_a_perimeter_written_again_by_another_vm_then_gone_is_said_for_each_vm`), a defect of the code, tracked in #492 (L1W); mutant M3442 (the journal clause taken off the machine) keeps the clause.

## [0.5.2] — 2026-11-03 — "kept apart"

### Security
- **runs/: one directory per writer, read-only for the others** (SEC18, #455, N-05; `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, « `runs/` — qui écrit, qui lit, qui monte »; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md` §2; `docs/operations.md`, « `runs/` : un répertoire par écrivain — le redéploiement coordonné »). runs/ was one directory every service mounted read-write: the red wrote where the blue writes its decisions, and read its reasoning.
  - **The layout** (`eregion_core/runs_layout.py`): `runs/glorfindel/` (the watch: its cycles; on H0, what it pulls from the channel; the campaigns' reports and triage), `runs/console/` (the console, Glorfindel's CLI, the bot: `manual_actions.jsonl`), `runs/annatar/`, `runs/gothmog/` (each injector's signals, reports, windows; Annatar's campaigns), `runs/orodruin/` (on Orodruin: the journal before each call, the lock of « one run at a time », the requests' statuses). Each writer writes only its own, by SEC16's held writes — the injectors' signals, windows, journal and registry now too (`append_line`, `write_file`; `append_line` gains `sync`).
  - **The mounts**: each service mounts its directory read-write, those it reads read-only, nothing else — compose, the Makefile's docker runs (`ANNATAR_VOLS`, `GLORFINDEL_VOLS`) and Orodruin's (`ORO_RUN` for Annatar, `ORO_RUN_GOTHMOG`, `ORO_RUN_CONTRACT`, `ORO_RUN_REQUEST`, which launches both). No service mounts runs/ whole; the red mounts neither the watch's directory nor the console's.
  - **Annatar no longer reads the watch's files**: a run's detection is measured through the return channel of the storage (`EREGION_WATCH_CHANNEL`), which keeps the priority, or, without it, through **the local channel of verdicts**, `runs/verdicts/` (decision of Jonathan, 03/11: the local purple loop keeps its measure). The watch alone writes it (`watch_channel.LocalVerdicts`, the Publisher's rule plus the poll of a run's `attack_started`), the red mounts it read-only — the one directory of the blue it sees. A verdict holds exactly `run_id`, `technique`, `verdict`, `detected_at`, `rule`, `latency_s`, `target` (the heartbeat: `at`, `since`, `windows`, `perimeter`), anything else refused at the write and at the read; a verdict of another run, or of another shape, is ignored and said. With neither channel the detection is `unknown`, said, never a miss. Not on Orodruin (`migrate --orodruin`). `annatar campaign run` without a channel to H0 no longer writes the campaign's report: it says the gesture (`make campaign-report`).
  - **The readers**: the watch takes signals from Annatar's directory only, the console and the analysis each file from its writer's directory only (the windows from every injector's and the watch's; a campaign's report written to the watch's directory, whoever wrote the campaign: `runs_layout.campaign_output`). A file anywhere else — the former layout, an injector's signal in the console's directory — is never read, and said once (the watch every minute, the console, `make runs-survey`). A run's report in an injector's directory must name that injector (`report_refusal`). The watch purges windows in its own directory only.
  - **A writer's directory that is a link is never made, moved through nor mounted** (the review): Docker resolves a bind's source on the host, so a link left in `runs/annatar` would hand its target to its writer. `migrate` stops before any move (exit 3), `RUNS_DIRS`, `ORO_DIRS` and `deploy.sh` refuse to start; below, a link or a pipe is left and said; a file moves by a hard link, never over another. A report in an injector's directory must be of its kind (an attack for Annatar, a fault for Gothmog), a window likewise (`window_refusal_of`, the watch and the console). The services stop before a migration that has something to move.
  - **The migration**: the former layout (files flat in runs/) is never read; it is moved once, on the host, before the services start — `make glorfindel-start`/`-restart`/`team-start`/`team-init`, H0's `deploy.sh`, `make orodruin-push` (`ORO_MIGRATE`, the one container that mounts `~/runs` whole) — to its writer's directory when the writer is told without ambiguity (its name, what it says of itself, a marker of the pull), else left, said with the gesture; nothing is deleted. `make runs-migrate`, `make runs-survey`.
  - **For Jonathan: the coordinated redeployment, in order** — the workstation, H0, Orodruin (`docs/operations.md`). On H0, **before publishing**: `make glorfindel-host-cd-install`, `-cd-check`, `-cd-enable` from an up-to-date `main` — the CD switches with its own installed `deploy.sh`, and the one from before SEC18 does not migrate (docker would make the writers' directories as root). Then: the Makefile of SEC18 against Orodruin's image from before stops every injector at its window, before its target, until `make orodruin-push`; a rollback of H0 below SEC18 no longer sees what the migration moved.
  - Tests: `test_runs_layout.py`, `test_runs_mounts.py` (the compose file, the Makefile's docker runs, H0's `deploy.sh`, Orodruin's module), and the readers' and writers' tests moved to the layout; mutants M3220–M3239, M3320–M3329 (the local channel), M3330–M3339 (the review), `test_local_verdicts.py`, and M61, M69, M74, M114, M738, M1231, M1422, M1918, M2849, M2954, M3009, M3010, M3145 re-anchored.

### Added
- **What a human login does as root, read in auditd's lines — the root shell's blind spot** (AUD1, #408; `docs/design/correlation.md`, « Ce que ferme AUD1 »; `docs/operations.md`, « auditd au banc (AUD1) »). Under sudo, what is done in a root shell is never written: ADM1 and COR1 judge sudo's line only, and COR2 kept the rest as a limit. auditd keeps the login identity (`auid`) through sudo and su. On the bench, behind a Terraform variable off by default (`auditd_enabled`, in `terraform.tfvars`; `infra/terraform/auditd.tf`, never applied here): a CustomScript extension on each bench VM (`scripts/auditd.sh`, never the `custom_data`) loads the CIS benchmarks' rule (`-C euid!=uid -F auid!=unset -S execve -k user_emulation`) and the one it does not cover — after sudo, a root shell has uid = euid = 0 — (`-F euid=0 -F auid>=1000 -F auid!=unset -S execve -k root_command`), the ENRICHED format and the syslog plugin (`audisp-syslog`, local6), and the bench's DCR collects `local6` into `Syslog`: the same table and reading as ADM1 and COR1, rather than an agent text log into a custom table. `make celebrimbor-auditd-plan` / `celebrimbor-auditd-up` plan and apply the extension and the DCR alone. A shipped rule, `auditd-sensitive-root-command` (T1059.004), gives one row per exec of a human login (auid ≥ 1000, never unset) as root, with every SYSCALL and EXECVE record of the event; the RulePoller reads it before any signal (`glorfindel/auditd.py`): a command of ADM1's sensitive list — the same list, never a copy (`expected_sudo.listed`), an expected administrator held to it as for sudo — makes a card that names the login account, its command and why; any other command none; a row unreadable or ambiguous (a record missing, two for one event — a line written at the event's serial never chooses what is read —, an argument cut, a number out of bounds, a command too long, a reading that raises) a card that says why (A0). A fault of a reading step of the poller (AUD1, ADM1, COR1) is said in `last_error` and the pass goes on with the rows as read; a poll that raises no longer kills the rule's thread. The program is read by its path, argv[0] being the caller's; one card per VM and poll names the others; the rule's window is kept in `rule_status.json` and said by `glorfindel report`. Stopping or killing `audisp-syslog` or `audispd` by name is now sensitive too. The records' form, the volume (~3 KB per exec, under 1 USD a month on the bench) and the noise are hypotheses A1–A13, measured on the bench with Jonathan. What stays open: what is not an exec (a shell builtin, a redirection), a renamed binary, an exec without a human's auid (a daemon, Run Command, root's cron), the lines' silence. Tests `tests/unit/test_auditd.py`, `tests/unit/test_bench_auditd.py`, `infra/terraform/tests/auditd.tftest.hcl`; the KQL subset reads several aggregations (`min`, `countif`, `take_any`, `make_set_if`) and `tolong`; mutants M2760–M2779.
- **Dagorlad's drills: kept, laid again, played again** (DG-d, #350; `docs/dagorlad.md`, « Garder et rejouer un drill »; `docs/design/console.md` §7, « Les drills »). A drill is attacks and faults drawn towards targets, kept under a name — an exercise repeated to keep the last proof under 30 days. Its run stays a request, each of its wires a run.
  - **The format, in the shared core** (`eregion_core/drill.py`, D1): a name, an author (`operator`, `annatar`, `eregion`), an optional description and time, 1 to 20 wires — an experiment and a target. One exact reader. **Never a resource id**: it changes when Celebrimbor rebuilds the bench.
  - **In layers, as the detection rules**: Eregion's shipped drills in `drills/` (`bl1-reponse`: BL1's fault then T1136.001 on the bench; `sentinelle`: T1136.001 on an enrolled VM of the production zone — the empty sentinel, for one), reviewed in a PR, copied into the console's image, aiming **by zone**; the instance's in the console's own directory (`~/.eregion/console/drills/`), aiming **by name**, never where the watch reads. An instance's drill named as a shipped one replaces it there, said; an unreadable file is listed with why.
  - **In Dagorlad**: a DRILLS line between the board and the plan; « Save as drill » keeps the wires drawn (`POST /api/dagorlad/drills`, behind SEC1: the token, the origin, a JSON body) by the names of their VMs, enrolled and read now, over a drill of the same name only on « Replace it »; the list, « Load », « YAML » (the export, for Git, the twin, or a PR to `drills/`). **Loading starts nothing**: it lays wires on the board.
  - **Targets are never chosen in the operator's place** (Jonathan's decision, 03/11): a shipped drill's wire lays « to place », with a button per enrolled VM of its zone and « all », and when each last ran the technique here — a hint, never a choice; the request is not written while a wire waits. **A name that several enrolled VMs bear** (a twin copied from a bench keeps its names) takes none: never kept by « Save as drill », laid « to place » with each VM said by its group, refused by the command.
  - **From the operator's machine**: `make orodruin-exercise DRILL=<name> [TARGETS=<vm>,…|all]` — the instance's drill (H0, or `REQUEST_FROM=local`), else the shipped one; its wires laid on the enrolled VMs read by Azure with the operator's identity; a shipped drill without `TARGETS` is refused, its candidates listed; each wire judged by `exercise_plan`; then as `REQUEST=` (shown, names typed, `[y/N]`, Orodruin judging again). An instance's drill that is a link is refused, never followed nor replaced by the shipped one. `DRILL`, `TARGETS` and `DRILL_VMS` (a listing of the VMs read elsewhere than Azure — the tests, a machine without access —, said) come from the command line only.
  - Tests: the reader, the layers, the save, the placing, a drill kept, reloaded, judged and played again on Orodruin's fake (`test_drill.py`), the console's routes and page (`test_console_dagorlad_drills.py`), the command (`test_makefile_exercise.py`); the captures redone with `drills.png`; mutants M3300–M3319, M3372–M3379; M2016 re-anchored.

### Fixed
- **A partial release run again never says « verified » while the quarantine NSG it left keeps its rules** (I3V, #450, found by I3U's review; `docs/azure.md`, « Une levée partielle relancée » ; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3V »). After a `release_partial`, the CLI says to run `glorfindel release` again, and the console's approval runs that command. It asks `verify_release` first, which looked only at the NSGs the NICs carry now: a NIC given its NSG back, its pair left on the quarantine NSG it left, it said verified — « No active isolation found on NSG — state cleared », exit 0, the state deleted, the release (which knew where to look, `quarantines_left`) never run. One fault was enough. `verify_release` now reads where the release looks on NSGs a NIC no longer carries: the quarantine NSG it left (`quarantines_left`, a quarantine placement) and the NSG of a placement by rules of the state; a rule of that NIC there is still isolated, named with its NSG, an unreadable NSG unknown. The command then runs the release: the pair taken off (`released`), or `release_partial`, exit 2, the state kept. `reset --from-azure` reads the same NSGs and takes the pair off. `glorfindel reset --dry-run` said « ✓ Reset complete — <vm> is clean » on a preview: it says « DRY RUN — aucune modification » and what the reset would do. `release.py` and `sweep.py` split first, nothing changed (`gone.py`, `reset_bare.py`). I3R's two frozen sequences no longer reached I3R's world since I3C (they passed with the release's sweep blind to the quarantine NSG): their world rebuilt, red again without I3R's fix. Tests: a rule of the invariant machine (`release_by_the_cli`; the oracle unchanged, A0), one frozen sequence (two variants, red on `main` 1a3f6a3), eleven unit tests in `test_release_away.py` (ten red on `main`); mutants M3192–M3198, seventeen re-anchored by the split.
- **A block of unknown port no longer keeps the port of an earlier block** (L1P, #478, found by the CI of PR #476; `docs/azure.md`, « Blocage gradué par port »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1P »). The same IP blocked again merges into its entry; a block written whole now sets the entry's threat port — None (unknown) too, which reads every port as the threat's. Kept, the 22 of an earlier SSH block judged a later block of any port on port 22 alone: an allow of 443 before our deny read as an « exposure », the block verified, its bypass never said (L1). A partial block adds only a port it knows (held by its mark, the next block written whole sets its own). Every other reader of the port already took None for every port: `verify_block_ip` (a VM's placements, the perimeter alone, a perimeter that holds, what is left of a pair), `_shadowing_rules`, the reassertion's put-back (the entry's own port); the console and the CLI show each placement's `threat_port_open` as written at its block — a placement an earlier block left, the new one not rewriting it, keeps its judgement of then (display only). Tests: the CI's sequence frozen (22 rules as found, two by hand), `test_glorfindel.py` (`…written_again_is_judged_on_its_own_port`, `…partial_block_adds_only_the_port_it_knows`, `…perimeter_block_of_unknown_port…`), `test_reassert.py` (the put-back with its port, None too); mutants M3393–M3399.
- **An acknowledged drift card holds only while none of its rules is written again or more gone** (L1G, #461; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1G »). Two older L1s of one family, red on `main`: « the block vanished from Azure; the reassertion neither put it back nor said so ». The check of a block now says each rule of its entry as it read it (`rules_seen`: what it does, or absent — from the listings it made, no read more), and the card keeps it (`providers/azure/verify_block.py`, `glorfindel/reassert_said.py`). It is the reassertion's alone: kept out of a cycle's outcome (what the model sees among its past cycles) and out of the console's record of an approved action (`response.policy.as_recorded`) — nothing the model sees changes.
  - **Seed 29 at 60**: web2 blocked on its subnet and for itself (one merged entry), its rules and its pair gone, said, acknowledged; then web1 blocked on the same subnet — its perimeter block writes the pair web2's entry names too. web2's own rules still gone, « better than the card, not worse »: unsaid. A rule the card read absent, written again under our name, now ends the episode of an acknowledged card, as a block written again does (L1Q): what is still gone is put back once, or said. A rule set right by hand without being deleted still says nothing (RB1); under a pending card, what comes back narrows the baseline, as before. The first time decides the same (`still_said`, `reassert_perimeters.py`).
  - **L1Qs' `xfail`**: a block's rules still on Azure, aimed at an address its NIC no longer has, are named « gone »; said (« le reposer a échoué », writes refused), acknowledged, then deleted — gone by name both times, the deletion read « no worse ». A rule the card read there, now absent, is a rule more gone: said again. The `xfail` is lifted.
  - **The seed's instability was the machine's** (`FlakyStrategyDefinition`, a pass one run in two): `operator_acknowledges` sorted the pending cards by their ids, a uuid4 drawn anew at every run — the drawn index fell on web2's card or web1's by chance. The cards are taken in the store's order now (`_reassert_block_cards`), the rule's draws unchanged; the race's interleaving was always the seed's. The sequence is frozen as found (thirteen rules) and shrunk to five, red on `main` every run.
- **An orphan rule never keeps a live VM cut because it also cuts a VM Glorfindel holds** (U1G, #463, L1Qs' seed 10 at 500; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Règles orphelines »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « U1G »). A deny of a deleted NIC with two addresses cut two live VMs: one blocked or isolated by Glorfindel, one held for nothing, given the other address since. The held VM shielded the whole rule (`find_orphan_rules`, `_held_reason`: its NIC recreated, the rule may be its isolation) — the other stayed cut by a rule nobody owns (U1). The rule is now **narrowed** to the held VMs' addresses (`glorfindel/providers/azure/narrow.py`): read again by its name before the write — still a deny, its addresses those listed, else left and said —, never deleted whole (the held VM's flows unchanged); a value that is not one exact address (`*`, a range, a tag) keeps it whole, as before. The guards before the write (a VM held since, a turned rule behind it) and the mode (D-A) are those of the VMs it frees. In `human_only`, the card says `az network nsg rule update … --destination-address-prefixes …`, « jamais la retirer entière ». The strict `xfail` L1Qs froze is lifted (its last reassertion's draw added: the sequence stopped at the failure). Oracle unchanged (A0). Mutants M3290–M3299; M760, M778, M779, M1741 re-anchored.
- **The reassertion of the blocks in two times; a perimeter block reads the others'** (L1Qs, #424, found by L1Qr's review; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Réaffirmation »; `docs/azure.md`, « Un blocage fusionné »; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « L1Qs »). The invariant machine gains rules — none removed nor narrowed, the oracles unchanged with one mode for every VM (A0): Db--01's primary NIC on the data subnet (its perimeter there, beside one of the app subnet's), a pass with a mode drawn per VM or a VM's lock held by another writer, two perimeters on two subnets, a perimeter pair turned and left out of the pass beside a neighbour rewritten, then the card acknowledged and the perimeter lifted; I5 extends VM by VM, and I3 gains « never said reached where our perimeter cuts it off ». What it found, each frozen, red on `main`, fixed in Glorfindel's code:
  - **A NIC whose subnet couldn't be read is never named off the perimeter it sits behind** (I3G's seed 9): a 429 on that read left the NIC's own NSG alone in its view, and the perimeter block said « the IP still reaches it » (`blocked_partial`). `_off_perimeter` now raises: the NICs are said unread, the check can't tell (`providers/azure/perimeter.py`).
  - **The perimeter alone reads another VM's perimeter** (seeds 5 and 8 at 60): a NIC off its subnet that another entry's perimeter cuts off was said « not covered, the IP reaches it ». `verify_block_ip` asks `_perimeter_holds`: held, it holds; unreadable, unknown (`providers/azure/verify_block.py`).
  - **Two entries holding a perimeter, on two subnets, a VM behind each, wait for each other**: read first, one rewrote its pair and read itself again with the other still gone — « n'est plus bloquée » of a NIC the other put back a moment later; no order suits both. First time (`glorfindel/reassert_perimeters.py`): every pair the second time would put back, of every entry, put back and marked — decided the same way (nothing in `human_only`, nothing put back once already, nothing said and no worse, nothing an unblock holds). Second time: each entry read and finished — its VM's rules, the re-read, the card.
  - **A drift better than its card narrows its baseline from its first read, before the entry's writes** (seed 1 at 500, before the two times): a write refused on a full disk earlier in the pass left the baseline as the card said, and the rules gone again read « not worse », unsaid (L1). An entry the first time writes is finished by the second from the first time's read, through the same prologue (the narrowing, the check's card, the bypass) — skipped at first, found by the 5.5 review. Cost: a perimeter entry not written is read twice per pass. A watch cut between the two times leaves pairs put back and marked without their « reposé une fois » card: the next disappearance is said « a second time », never unsaid.
  - Measured, nothing to fix: a perimeter pair turned and left out of the pass, the neighbour rewritten beside it, its card acknowledged, the perimeter lifted — what opens is said each time. Two older defects, red on `main`, out of this lot, frozen as strict `xfail` and routed: U1 (seed 10 at 500, an orphan `-out` rule left on nsg-tier) and L1 (the harness's unmutated run, a block under writes refused, its address changed). Mutants M2990–M2999 and M3150–M3152 (the reserve, three); M1648, M1649, M2194, M1943, M2155, M3120 re-anchored.
- **An operation on a VM never cuts off another through an older isolation turned into an allow** (I4G, #460, found by I3V's measure, seed 73; `docs/azure.md`, « Le refus total d'une ancienne version, retourné en autorisation » ; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I4G »). The guard every removal by name asks (`_held_by_turned`, I4B) knew our rules by two prefixes, `glorfindel-iso-` and `glorfindel-block-`. An older version's isolation is named `glorfindel-isolation-deny-all` (`-out`, or VM-suffixed): turned into an allow by someone, it was no turned rule of ours, the guard answered « nothing to keep » without reading it, and the release of that older isolation — its reset too — took it off by its name. A VM given that NSG since lost what the allow let in (on `*`: every NIC behind the NSG). A rule under these names (`turned._OLDER_NAMES`), turned into an allow, is now read by the guard when its removal by that name is asked: the allow stays and is said (« gardée : notre règle glorfindel-isolation-deny-all, retournée en autorisation par quelqu'un… »), `release_partial` or `swept_partial`, the state kept; the deny of the pair goes. What to do is never to put it back as a deny — on an NSG shared since, a deny-all (or any turned rule on `*`) would cut every NIC behind it, the VM it opens too — but to take it off by hand if no longer wanted, or recreate the flow under another VM's own name, then run again. These names do not count among the turned rules a deny holds back (I5Q, `_TURNED_NAMES`): there, a name homonyms or a customer can carry kept a true orphan that cuts a live VM (U1, found by the review). Tests: `tests/unit/test_turned_names.py` (18), the frozen sequence `test_an_older_isolations_release_never_cuts_off_the_vm_its_turned_deny_all_lets_in` (red on `main` 5482fc1, the oracle unchanged); mutants M3270–M3275.

## [0.5.1] — 2026-11-03 — "every alert said"

### Added
- **The origin of a session confirmed outside the VM** (COR2, #427; `docs/design/correlation.md`, « L'origine confirmée hors de la VM »; `docs/operations.md`, « Déclarer la source qui confirme l'origine sur H0 (COR2) »). COR1 sets aside an expected administrator's root shell when every SSH session of the account comes from a declared origin — read in the VM's Syslog, which any local user writes (`logger -t sshd`). A new key, `expected_sudo.origin_proof` (`none` by default, `bastion`, `flows`; read strictly: another value, or a source with no entry declaring `origins`, refuses the file), names a source outside the VM: each of those sessions must then also have its trace there — an Azure Bastion session to that VM (`MicrosoftAzureBastionAuditLogs`: `TargetResourceId`, `UserName`, `SessionStartTime` within 120 s; one Bastion session confirms one SSH session, never two), or an allowed flow from its address to the VM's port 22 (`NTANetAnalytics`, traffic analytics). The VM is the registry's, never the line's; the values are KQL literals; one bounded query per VM (back to the oldest session, 1,000 rows), within COR1's cap of 4 per rule and poll, only for the root shells COR1 would set aside. A trace absent is a card (« … sans trace dans Azure Bastion — origine non confirmée hors de la VM », counted `unconfirmed`); a source unreadable, cut, capped, a VM it cannot name, an unreadable instant: unknown, a card (A0). Set aside with its proof, it is journaled (`proof`) and counted (`confirmed`, `origin_confirmed`); `glorfindel report` and the console say « every one confirmed outside the VM (COR2) », or « origin read in the VM only ». Without a source, COR1 is unchanged. A source lifts the forged line's part of COR1's limit, not the rest: a session older than the window, an access that writes no session line, stay unseen (AUD1, #408). The tables' columns and latencies are hypotheses from Microsoft Learn, to be measured on the bench (B1–B5, F1–F4). Threat model: #427 held when a source is declared. **New key**: a version without COR2 refuses a config that carries it. `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `few_shot_examples.yaml` and `_build_user_message()` untouched. Tests: `test_correlation.py` (the queries run as written over Bastion and flow rows in memory, `kql_subset` gains `=~`); mutants M3040–M3059, M3160–M3161.

### Fixed
- **Every mutant measured: the harness's `__main__` guard last** (MCG, #448; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « Tous les mutants mesurés »). `python scripts/mutation_check.py` (the Monday measure, `--only`) stops at its `if __name__ == "__main__"` guard, and merges had left blocks after it: 54 mutants (M2880–M2899, M2940–M2959, M3030–M3036, M3075–M3081, from SEC11 and SEC14) were never measured, and `scripts/mutant_anchors.py`, which imports the module, said nothing. The blocks move above the harness's functions, nothing lost (the same top-level statements, compared by `ast`; the same `MUTANTS`, `CONTROL` and `TESTS` once imported), and the guard is the file's last statement. `tests/unit/test_mutant_anchors.py` fails when the harness run as a script defines fewer mutants than an import, or when a statement follows the guard; `mutant_anchors.py` counts it as a fault and names its mutants. A new block goes before the guard (`docs/sessions.md`). The 54, measured: 54 caught in every round (2 rounds). Mutants M3185–M3186.
- **A VM's autonomy mode no longer depends on the case of its name** (AUC, #447; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Modes d'autonomie par asset »; `docs/operations.md`, « Configuration »). `autonomy.resolve` compared a VM's name to `autonomy.assets` by `fnmatch.fnmatch` and by equality, both case-sensitive on Linux, whereas Azure ignores the case of a VM's name: an entry `human_only` written `VM-PROD-DB` did not hold the VM its state names `vm-prod-db`, which took the default — `non_disruptive` when it was. The names and patterns are now compared case ignored (`fnmatchcase` on both, lowercased), the exact entry first, then the longest pattern, then the default, as before; entries tied (`VM-A` and `vm-a`, two patterns as long) that disagree give the most prudent mode, no longer the first listed. The console's switch (`set_asset_mode`) updates every entry that names the VM exactly, case ignored, its spelling kept — no longer the first one only, nor a second one beside it. `exceptions[].asset_pattern` is matched the same way (`expected_sudo.admins[].match` already was, ADM1; accounts keep their case). No key changes. An entry written with its VM's case reads as before. **An entry written with another case, without effect until now, takes effect both ways**: `human_only` at last holds the VM, but a `non_disruptive` entry makes it autonomous (after readiness) and an exception takes it out of the watch — `vm-?: non_disruptive` under a `human_only` default now makes `VM-A` autonomous. The names compared are Azure's: the end of ARM's `resource_id`, with the case it was created with, for the mode; the Heartbeat's `Computer` for the exceptions. Read `exceptions` and `autonomy.assets` against the VMs' names as Azure gives them before updating H0. Tests: `test_config.py`; mutants M3180–M3184.
- **SEC12, after the switch of 02/11** (`docs/sessions.md`, « Basculer les sessions »; `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`). The `gh` of the agent sessions is now a dispatcher in `~/.local/bin/gh`, read at each command: Claude Code builds its shell before applying the project's `env` block, so the `~/.zshrc` condition never saw `EREGION_AGENT_SESSION` (measured). The tag rule is read without a token by H0 (measured: « 🔒 … réservé par la règle « tags v* » »), and `make glorfindel-host-cd-status` shows it — `cd-check` does not when the release already runs; after `cd-install`, `cd-enable` (the timer is left disabled). `#releases` no longer says the CD deploys a pre-release (`scripts/notify_discord.py`). Tests: `test_notify_discord.py`.
- **An alert of the reassertion is never folded into a card of the graph** (I3G, #446, found by I3C's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3G »). The escalation store folded a new card into a pending one of the same action, resource (any case), type and IP, whatever its signal id: the graph's `verification_failed` card on a VM — the check after its action failed —, still pending, took in the reassertion's alert that the VM's isolation (block) vanished. `_alert` closes only the reassertion's own cards and `drift_said` reads only them: in `human_only` the disappearance was said again at every pass in the report (`occurrences` up to 5), never notified, the graph's card overwritten at the first fold (the confidence moved from 0.9 to 0); outside it, the card stayed « re-applied once » and the second disappearance (« not re-applied ») was folded in, its text unchanged, no notification — the VM no longer isolated: an alert gone silent. The other way, the graph's re-fire overwrote a pending alert's text, its id still read « said ». The store now never folds a card of the reassertion's (`reassert-…`, `state-unreadable-…`, any case) into a card of another origin, nor the reverse (`escalations._reassertion`): each alert has its own card, its text and its notification, and the graph's card keeps its own. Chosen over closing the graph's card first: one rule at the store covers every card of the reassertion (drift, bypass, check that raised, rules left, unreadable state) and both directions, and never closes a card the operator has not read. In passing, the former name-only key (`reassert-{vm}-{ip}`, before A0) is compared in lowercase: its card for an IPv6 in capitals is closed by the next alert. A rule of the invariant machine (`graph_card_before_a_disappearance`) and a new property, L3 (the oracles unchanged, A0: L1 counted any card of the action), a frozen sequence red on `main`, unit tests; mutants M3170–M3176. The console grouped a VM's cards by action alone: the graph's card and the alert showed as one group, under the first one's title, and one « Ack » acknowledged both — the alert silenced unread. Its group key now carries the origin, told as the store tells it (`_fromReassertion`), and each « Ack » acknowledges one group's ids (M3177); the bot and the TUI acknowledge card by card.
- **A release on a state turned unreadable never says « released »** (I3U, #440; `docs/azure.md`, « Un état devenu illisible » ; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3U »). Found by I3R's review: a partial release keeps in its state what the retry must still look for — the quarantine NSG left (`quarantines_left`), the placements kept. That state turned unreadable since (a disk, a write cut short), the retry read nothing, found the NICs on their own NSG, said `released` and **deleted the unreadable file** — the pair left on the quarantine NSG, the only record of where to look gone. Now the release does what it can (the rules found by their names are taken off, as before), says `release_partial` and why (« état local illisible (isolation/<file>) … levée non vérifiée — le réparer, ou le retirer à la main après avoir cherché les règles glorfindel-iso de ses cartes, puis relancer »), said once, and keeps the file as it is; repaired, the retry takes the normal path. The same for every reader of that state: `reset --from-azure` says `swept_partial` (its preview too), `verify_release` is not verified — `glorfindel release`, which asks it first, said « No active isolation found on NSG — state cleared » and deleted the file —, and `glorfindel reset` without `--from-azure` no longer says « Nothing to reset » (it read the readable states only): it runs the release, exit 2. `_clear_isolation_state` never deletes an unreadable file, whoever asks (the AWS adapter keeps it, but its release still says `released` on it — open). The way out names where to look: before removing the file, the NICs' rules on `nsg-glorfindel-quarantine-<region>` (its group: the configured one, else the VM's) — not on the NSGs they carry now, where an operator would find nothing; that warning is said even when a NIC's message already names the file (I3U's review). Tests: one sequence frozen through the invariant machine (two variants, red on `main` 4f0c3a5 and, its world set as seed 67 left it once I3C closed the path that led there, on `main` f20d3c8, by the unchanged oracle), nine unit tests in `test_release_away.py` (red on `main`); mutants M3131–M3135, M3190–M3191, M1253 re-anchored, M1254's test given another VM's unreadable state (the VM's own now said by the release).
- **The reassertion finds its cards whatever the case of the VM's name** (I3C, #436, found by I3R's review; `docs/design/preuves.md`, « I3C »). The ids of its cards carried the VM's name as the state gave it, and a VM isolated or blocked again by its lowercase id (Log Analytics' `_ResourceId`) has its state name it in lowercase: the new alert neither closed the card said under the canonical name nor was read « already said » — the store folded it into that card, which kept its id and its text. In `human_only` the disappearance was said again at every pass; outside it, put back after it was said, « re-applied » folded into a card saying it would not be. With `web` (no capital) it held. The ids are written in lowercase now (drift, bypass, check that raised, rules left), and every search of them reads them whatever their case (`reassert_cards.same_sid`, the operator's word, the closure of the cards whose VM is no longer held). The whole id is lowercased, the IP too: a block of an IPv6 written in capitals (`2001:DB8::9`) had its check card closed and made again at every pass, its bypass said again (found by the lot's review, M3165–M3168). The cards an older version left pending under the canonical name are found — closed by the next alert, never left orphaned. A rule of the invariant machine (`taken_up_by_its_lowercase_id`; the oracle unchanged, A0), a frozen sequence that checks the cards on the store (red on `main`: the machine's oracles don't count a card said twice), unit tests; mutants M3100–M3109, and M37, M1259, M1765, M1930, M1931, M1933, M1936, M1937, M1941, M1948, M2931 re-anchored.

### Security
- **The rest of the second counter-audit** (SEC17, #451; `docs/design/modele-de-menace.md`, « L'état des constats bas du second contre-audit »).
  - **The memory has an age** (V-12; `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Source d'un cycle »). A cycle is dated when it is stored (`stored_at`), by the host's clock, never by what it carries; the search gives only the real cycles (SEC4a) stored less than `memory.max_age_days` ago — 90 days by default; a new config key read strictly: a finite number of days, positive, at most 3650 (`docs/operations.md`, « Configuration »). A real cycle stored before SEC17 has no date of its own: it is dated by **B2's floor**, 2026-10-16 00:00 UTC (`B2_FLOOR`, decision of Jonathan, 03/11) — a cycle is `real` only since B2 (d4e51fa, 16/10), `store` is the only writer, a cycle without `source` is never given: no real cycle is older, so its age is at least its true one, never today's nor a date it carries. It is given while that floor is within the bound: on H0, with 90 days, the current memory is kept until about 2027-01-14, then ages out. Nothing is erased: `glorfindel report` (and `memory-stats`) says the real cycles by age — within the bound, older — and apart those dated by B2's floor. `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, `few_shot_examples.yaml` and `_build_user_message()` unchanged; the model is given fewer past cycles (an end-to-end run with BL1, #280).
  - **The watch's heartbeat written at once**: beside it, renamed over it, in the mode it had (0644) — the injectors' gate, the console, the CD of H0 never find it empty or cut. So are the other state files another process reads and that were still written in place: a run's report in runs/ (`RunReport.save`, SEC16's `write_file`) and the console's restores in progress (under a file lock, 0600).
  - **The channel's token goes to the declared account only** (N-15). The account is already declared: the two channels' URLs and, on Orodruin, their containers' scopes its contract reads (`EREGION_CHANNEL_SCOPE`, `EREGION_WATCH_CHANNEL_SCOPE`), all Terraform outputs of `infra/orodruin`. A URL of another account, or declarations that disagree (the two channels on two accounts), are refused and said before a token is asked for; a scope naming no account is no absent declaration.
