A control-plane rule looking back less than 30 minutes, or not projecting `CorrelationId` (two operations at the same instant would fold into one), is said at load. **A control-plane detection is decided by the code, never by the model** (`_control_plane_decision`, on `context.plane` or the source — `respond` too — and, in depth, on the row's `OperationNameValue`: an AzureActivity row read by a rule of another source): no `investigate` on the anchor VM, no call to the model (it would only add text, cost, and send an administrator's name to the provider), **no action recommended**; escalation `control_plane_change` whose reason gives the operation, its status as read (an operation seen at `Start` may fail afterwards: said), the caller, its address, the target, the grave marks and the severity; never approvable in one click, never folded into the previous card, no button nor command on the VM in Discord (nor on `unattributed_signal`). An unattributed control-plane signal opens no incident on its anchor VM, and no control-plane cycle enters the decision memory (ChromaDB). `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`, the few-shot, `_build_user_message` and the data-plane path are unchanged. The `snapshot-by-unexpected-caller` rule rests on a **hypothesis, to measure on the bench**: Azure Backup (Glorfindel's snapshot, a `glorfindel restore`) may produce `disks/beginGetAccess` or `snapshots/write`. `AzureActivity` is **not** in the workspace by default: a diagnostic setting on the subscription sends it there — `infra/terraform/activity_log.tf` proposes it for the bench, disabled (category Administrative only — `Security` is read by no rule; disabled, not even the subscription is read). The replay of a real window of the bench, read only, is left to do with Jonathan. Tests: `tests/unit/test_activity_logs.py` (a fake AzureActivity, no Azure, no LLM); mutants M810–M829, M850–M859, M890–M894. Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/azure.md`, `docs/operations.md`.
- **The analysis AI triages a campaign, with its own memory and model, and nothing applies without a human** (IA1, #88, 27/10, code side). `python -m analysis.triage runs/campaigns/<id>` writes `triage.md` and `triage.json`: what needs a human, computed by the code and sorted by a fixed priority — a missed technique first, then an unknown outcome, an escalation, a scenario in error, a detection slower than the catalog expects. Each item gathers what the code read: the runs (as the report reads them), the technique catalog (checked against its schema when `jsonschema` is there; an invalid one is not believed), Glorfindel's rules (the shipped base and the client's layer over it, read only) and the infrastructure code (is the technique's log source collected? Terraform read without its comments, the evidence cited as `file:line`; a source or a code it cannot read is « unknown », never « not collected »); and the causes of a miss (not in the catalog, no enabled rule, source not collected, collection unknown, or a rule and its source both there). The analysis model explains each item and proposes one of a closed set (`author_rule`, `review_rule`, `check_collection`, `review_decision`, `rerun`); the check removes an entry that names no item, a second one, a proposal the computed facts do not support (`author_rule` while a rule watches the technique…), anything that carries a rule (a KQL query, a rule in YAML), and on an unknown decision an explanation saying Glorfindel acted or did not. Every kept proposal is written `pending` by the code, whatever the model wrote; `python -m analysis.triage decide <campaign> <tri-N> approve|reject --by <name>` records a named human's decision, once, and nothing else — an approved proposal is work for a human, and a triage holding a decision is never written over. The analysis never uses Glorfindel's model nor its memory (`analysis/separation.py`): its model (`EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL`) is refused when it is Glorfindel's decision model, compared without case or provider prefix, and its index (`EREGION_ANALYSIS_INDEX`) when it lies in `~/.glorfindel`, path resolved — for the campaign report too; no module of `analysis/` imports Glorfindel or a way to run a command (a test reads the code). Five analysis cases for the harness (`analysis/triage_cases.py`: a miss without a rule, one whose source is collected only in a comment, one with rule and source, an escalation sorted after a miss, a planted line asking for a rule and an approval), run against the real model by `python scripts/analysis_eval.py` (Jonathan) and in the tests with a simulated model. After the review: the triage reads the client's layer as the watch does — an entry the watch skips (no query, `enabled` not a boolean, `interval_s: 30s`, a `ttp` or `name` not a string) no longer replaces the base rule, so a technique the watch still watches is never « no rule » (`tests/unit/test_analysis_rules_parity.py` compares with the watch's own loader on a table of layers); `decide` and the writing of a triage take a `flock` on `runs/campaigns/<id>/.triage.lock` — two decisions at once are both kept (tested across processes); an unreadable `triage.json` (not JSON, an unknown key, kind or status) is refused in one line, exit 1, nothing written and no model called; no turn of the model's prose is filtered (a regex on prose removed legitimate proposals such as « doit être validée par un humain »): what counts is the status the code wrote in `triage.json` — `pending`, or the decision `decide` recorded with the human's name — shown in bold beside each proposal in `triage.md`, whose header says that no decision is taken by the model; the model's text is quoted as its text, on one line (whatever ends a line — `\r` alone, `\r\n`, U+2028… — becomes a space), its Markdown escaped so that it cannot draw a status of its own (tested through a real CommonMark engine, markdown-it, on a table of hostile entries); what the check removed is shown on one line each, its reason written by the code and never quoting the model, escaped too; a new triage written while a decision is taken no longer loses it (the save takes the lock too); Annatar's container receives `GLORFINDEL_LLM_MODEL` (the end of `campaign run` writes its report there, and the refusal compared with the default); `host_env.py` copies `EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL` to H0's `host.env`, and `make glorfindel-host-model` says when the model chosen for Glorfindel is the analysis's (`python -m analysis.separation` in the watch exits 2: every report would be refused; 1 is an error, said apart; the target never fails on it); a refusal says what to set first, so the cut message of `campaign run` keeps it. Tests: `tests/unit/test_analysis_separation.py`, `tests/unit/test_analysis_triage.py`, `tests/unit/test_analysis_rules_parity.py`; mutants M780–M799, M840–M842, M844, M871–M878. Docs: `docs/analysis.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`, `docs/operations.md`, `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`.
- **Usage profiles, and each component runs alone in CI** (D1, #81, 27/10). Glorfindel is in every profile. The profiles are combinations, each with a page in `docs/profils/`:
  - Glorfindel alone, to discover in observation (`make profile-discover`);
  - Glorfindel and Annatar, « does my detection work? » (`make profile-detection`);
  - Glorfindel and Gothmog, « does my infrastructure hold? » (`make profile-resilience`);
  - everything, like H0 (`make profile-proven`);
  - one Glorfindel per team (`make team-init` and `make team-start TEAM=<name>`).

  **docker compose.** It gains two profiles, `annatar` and `gothmog`, for the injectors (`docker compose --profile annatar run --rm annatar …`):
  - each depends on the watch, reads its heartbeat in `~/.glorfindel` read-only, and shares its `runs/`;
  - each gets its own service principal (`ANNATAR_AZURE_CLIENT_*`), never Glorfindel's (block `x-injector`);
  - Orodruin's contract stays empty, so on a workstation an injector stays passive.

  **One instance per team.** Four variables name it: `EREGION_INSTANCE`, `EREGION_CONSOLE_PORT`, `EREGION_RUNS` and `EREGION_CONFIG`. Left empty, the file renders as before, the names H0 reads included (checked with `docker compose config`).
  - A team keeps all its state in `~/.eregion/teams/<name>/`: `team.env` (from `team.env.example`), its config, its runs, and the `HOME` compose reads.
  - `make team-start` runs compose under `env -i`, so the operator's shell (their subscription, their keys) never reaches a team. It refuses a team with no subscription or no console port.

  **The signal contract moved to the shared core.** It was `annatar/signals/schema.py` and is now `eregion_core/signals.py`. Glorfindel imported it from Annatar, so it could not run without Annatar's package.

  **Each component runs alone.** `scripts/component_check.py` imports and tests Glorfindel, Annatar, Gothmog and the core with the other components absent from the interpreter, and Glorfindel's dependencies absent for an injector. The CI does the same (`.github/workflows/components.yml`, which also runs `make profiles-check`), and so does `make components-check`.
  - Only the crossings listed with their reason are allowed. `tests/unit/test_components.py` refuses any other import of one component by another, and every test file must belong to a component or to `TOGETHER`.
  - The analysis AI (`analysis/`) is scanned too: the watch loads it (IA0). The campaign report reads a run's detections with Annatar's reader on load, a listed bridge, so a Glorfindel installed without Annatar starts with the campaign reports off and says so once: the watch imports the report before it starts their thread (M870).
  - `tests/unit/conftest.py` skips the fixtures of an absent component; a test checks that in the full suite none is skipped.
  - The profile targets create, as the operator, the directories the services mount (`~/.annatar` too): left to docker, they would be root's. `depends_on` starts the watch without waiting for its heartbeat, so in the first minute an injector may refuse (V1), as the pages now say. `team.env.example` carries the two channels, empty.
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` now opens on the adapters as the way in for contributors.

  Tests: `tests/unit/test_components.py`, `tests/unit/test_profiles.py` (the team targets run against a fake `docker`), `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`. Mutants M800–M809, M845–M849, M870. The harness no longer reads a failure of the component check as a broken copy, line by line: a module made absent on purpose never covers another missing one.
- **The console says what it runs, beside its logo** (#206, 27/10, Jonathan's request): the tag H0 deployed, else the package's version and the short commit (« v0.3.0 · 79f220f »); on hover, the full commit. `deploy.sh` passes the reference it builds from to the image (`EREGION_REF`, `EREGION_REVISION`, beside the labels `--show` reads); a local console reads its checkout's commit. In `/api/sections`. Mutants M566, M567.
- **The five indicators of the finality on the console's overview** (UI-2, #206, 27/10; definitions decided by Jonathan): time to contain (in human-only, « nothing contained alone », with the median times to detect and to decide), contained alone (the real share, and the shadow one: the detected attacks Glorfindel would have isolated or blocked at or above the gate, not judged wrong), disruptive actions judged wrong (30 days, 7 aside), coverage (detected techniques over the implemented ones and over the catalogue; faults aside) and the last proof (the most recent attack detected with a decision attached; green under 21 days, red over 30). `GET /api/indicators`, `eregion_console/indicators.py`: 30 days from the run reports, the watch's cycles and the cards — never from the exercise windows, emptied after 7; a run's decision is the watch's record at its attack time plus the latency it measured. Nothing invented. Mutants M560–M565.

### Changed
- **An orphan rule that cuts a VM off is taken off within the minute, by the VM's mode** (A3, #73, 27/10; U1 of the seventh review). A rule Glorfindel left for a NIC that no longer exists — a deleted VM's isolation or block, its state lost — stayed on a shared NSG, aimed at an address Azure hands to the next VM, which was cut off; L28 only alerted, at the posture cadence (30 min). The watch's reassertion pass now sweeps them **every minute, whatever discovery does** (`discovery.enabled: false` included), with the quarantine check of I1F and one listing of the NSGs for both (`glorfindel/orphans.py`). The rule is taken off only when the connector finds it removable — it cuts a live NIC that this NSG governs now (its own NSG or its subnet's), no NIC attached to the NSG is missing from the listing, a NIC of the local state with that hash is gone (a 404; a 403 or a 429 says nothing), and the VM it cuts is not one Glorfindel isolates or blocks (its NIC recreated by a `terraform apply`: a new id, the same address — the old NIC's rule is the isolation the state wants) — **and the VM it cuts runs in `non_disruptive` or `full_auto`** (the mode the operator gave it; the readiness hold doesn't apply: it asks whether a VM can be isolated and released, nothing this removal needs). Then the card is resolved with the reason. In `human_only`, **the card only, no write** (`human_only` only reads): it says the `az network nsg rule delete` command, or the mode that lets the watch do it. Each kept rule's card says why (`Gardée : …`), a failed removal says its error. A rule said is not said again while it stands (`~/.glorfindel/orphan_alerts.json`, as the quarantine alerts): its card stays pending, or acknowledged stays closed; it closes once the rule is no longer an orphan. The state is read again, under the cut VM's lock, just before the removal. Read-only credentials, or the reassertion turned off (`GLORFINDEL_REASSERT_INTERVAL_S=0`): the watch's pass doesn't run, the posture pass says them (the card only, never removed). A rule cutting several VMs is removed only when every one may act alone; an unreadable configuration is `human_only`; the held VM is matched by its id whatever the case. `AzureConnector.find_orphan_rules(nsgs=)` (`cut`, `removable`, `kept`), `remove_orphan_rule`, `nsg_listing`; `escalations.resolve(id, note=)`. Invariants: rule `orphan_sweep` (the cut VM's mode drawn), composite `address_reused`; at the end of every sequence each live VM is back to its design in full. The in-memory Azure's NICs now name their VM (`virtual_machine`), as Azure's do. Tests: `tests/unit/test_orphan_sweep.py`, a fixed sequence in `test_invariant_regressions.py`. Limits: the listing guard reads the NSGs attached to NICs, not a subnet's NSG over overlapping VNets; the machine doesn't recreate a NIC under a live VM (unit tests do).
- **`glorfindel-config.yaml` is read strictly** (A3). A key nobody reads, a value of the wrong type, `"false"` in quotes: the file is refused (`ConfigError`), every problem said at once at its path, with the key it resembles (`isolation.quarantaine_nsg : clé inconnue … « quarantine_nsg » ?`). Before, `quarantaine_nsg: false` left the quarantine on, `exclude_all: "false"` took the VM out of every rule (a non-empty string is true), `allow_destructive: "vm-*"` became the patterns `v`, `m`, `-` and `*`, `exclude_rules: ssh-brute-force` matched by substring. The watch no longer starts on a refused config (exit 2, the message in red); the isolation and the readiness already refused it (S1). A section left empty (its items commented out) is an empty list. A backend's `type` is one that something reads (`azure_monitor`, `prometheus`; `azure_backup_vault`): `type: azure_monitr` is refused with the one it resembles. The shipped example and Celebrimbor's fragment load. Docs: `docs/operations.md`.
- **The restore's replay check is an allowlist** (A3, L23). After the harmless Run Command, any VM extension but the agents that take no command (Azure Monitor, OMS/MMA, Dependency Agent, Diagnostics, Backup's VMSnapshot, Network Watcher, Entra ID sign-in, MDE, antimalware, Run Command's handlers; after the first review: Update Manager's patch and OS update extensions, Guest Attestation, Change Tracking, BGInfo — names as documented, to measure on the bench, which only has AMA) holds the release: VMAccess (it resets users and SSH keys), DSC, Chef, a third party's, one whose kind can't be read, and Guest Configuration (it applies what a policy assigns). It was a list of three CustomScript names. The card of a release held so names the extensions (`replay_vectors`, carried by `recovery_complete`), instead of blaming the last Run Command. Not in this lot (L23, #228): secrets passed by name (done by HY1, below) and the canary's checks (to do). Mutants M760–M779 and M880–M887 (M28's, M830's and M834's texts follow the code).
- **No visible text names the War Room any more** (#222, 27/10): the reason of a held `mode_hold` and the readiness card (« console, page Glorfindel : Review & turn on »), the suggested steps (« nœud DETECT de la console » — the MONITORING card no longer exists; « Approve on its card in the console » — there were never « War Room Incidents »), three CLI messages and the bot's startup warning. None of them reach the model: its memory keeps the technique, the action, its own reasoning and the outcome. `glorfindel --version` reads the package's version (it said 0.2.0, by hand). The README shows the console's overview, measured on the bench. Mutant M569.

### Fixed
- **A bare NIC whose original NSG is unknown stays unknown** (I8F, #227, 27/10, found by A3's run of the invariants on unmutated code — I8, 500 sequences): an isolation by quarantine whose state and tags were lost knew its NIC's original NSG no more (Q7); someone then took our NSG off the NIC and left it bare, and the next isolation recorded « no NSG » over that unknown — the release put no NSG back, said `released` and cleared the state, the NIC left without its own NSG, and an older version's isolation still on that NSG recorded nowhere. Such an isolation now keeps the original unknown (nothing written, neither in the state nor as a tag): the release keeps the NIC in quarantine and says which tag to set (`release_partial`), and `reset --from-azure`, which skipped the same bare NIC, now says it (`swept_partial`). The 186 steps reduce to four (`lose_everything`, `someone_detaches_the_quarantine`, `isolate`, `release`); its always-on 429 faults were the shrinking's leftovers — but a single 429 as the isolation reads our tag did the same harm (the error read as « no NSG », and written): an unreadable tag is now an unknown, nothing written — with the state lost and the tag intact, the error was written over a valid original, gone from Azure (second review); only the state's own explicit « none » holds. The oracle had a defect beside it: I8 counted the rules of a NIC's own NSG while the NIC was left bare with nothing tying it to that NSG any more (I3 and the blocks did not) — it no longer does, but only when the test itself lost the VM's state after it last saw a state hold that NIC in quarantine while the NIC was bare (its journal; a first version read the state the release started from, and a release dropping the placement it couldn't undo earned the exemption). I3 had the same defect (Glorfindel's own rules named for such a NIC, on its own NSG off it), and so had the state's consistency check (I8 of the reset, I11): the same exemption. A composite rule (`bare_with_an_unknown_original`) reaches the sequence: by the machine alone, at the push budget over three rounds, M860 2/3, M861 and M862 3/3, M863–M865 never (a 429 at the right call, two losses and two detachments stay rare); they are measured by their frozen sequences, then the machine. The oracle now sees a tag Glorfindel writes again after the test lost them: a stateless release that ignored it (M865) no longer passes. Regressions in `test_invariant_regressions.py`, mutants M860–M865.
- **The cards of I1F, AL1, C2 and A3 in the console** (#248, 27/10). `quarantine_altered`, `control_plane_change` and `identity_change` showed under their raw name (cards, details, TO JUDGE), `posture_gap` on its context line; each has its label and title, and a test now checks every type Glorfindel writes against the page (M570). **An anchored card** (`control_plane_change`, `identity_change`, `unattributed_signal`, the bot's `_ANCHORED_TYPES`, M571) is on a VM the detection does not name: no more « 🔄 Restore » nor `glorfindel restore` aimed at it (an unattributed detection recommending a restore offered it), only `glorfindel ack`, and the card says « ⚓ filed on …, not about it ». A detection-only card (AL1, C2) shows its title, its rule and its severity, `critical` in red, never a percentage; the live feed says « escalate → control plane » or « identity » instead of an empty « escalate → » (`held_by` passed, M573). The cards that are not decisions are Glorfindel's `_NOT_DECISIONS` (M572). **A card on an NSG** (a quarantine altered, an orphan rule no VM holds) has its own NSG tile, linked to PROTECT only, out of RECOVER's « unprotected » count — it used to be a VM tile with a mode, a backup and a Snapshot button (Jonathan's choice).
- **A quarantine NSG someone altered is said, even when the isolation's state is lost** (I1F, #226, 27/10, found by the deep run of the invariants — 10 000 sequences): a NIC in Glorfindel's quarantine whose state and NSG tags were lost was never read again — someone turning the quarantine's deny to Udp left the VM reachable on TCP from the VNet (Azure's default `AllowVnetInBound`), and nothing said so. At every interval of the reassertion pass, **whatever the local state** (the pass stopped before any Azure call when nothing was recorded), the watch now reads Glorfindel's quarantine NSGs — the whole subscription's (`list_all`), never only `isolation.quarantine_rg`'s: an NSG made in another group before the setting changed would go unseen — and raises one card per altered NSG holding NICs (`quarantine_altered`, resource = the NSG, high severity), not raised again once acknowledged while the change stands (`~/.glorfindel/quarantine_alerts.json`), closed by itself once the NSG is whole again, empty, or gone from the subscription's listing — never on an NSG read as unknown (unreadable configuration), nor when the listing fails; nothing is written (without a state, nothing says which VM nor its mode). The invariant failure itself (I1 at the very step of the alteration) was the test's: it read a deny of one protocol as a deny-all, so the opening was not counted as someone else's — the test now reads the protocol, grants that exemption only to the quarantine NSGs it altered itself (its journal, as on AWS), and checks the new liveness property L2 (altered → put back or said), with a composite rule that reaches it (`quarantine_altered_then_reasserted`). Regressions in `test_invariant_regressions.py`, mutants M830–M839, M895–M898.
- **The console finds the shipped detection rules wherever it is launched** (#205, 27/10, found by P1's review): since it left `glorfindel/`, the console looked for `detection_rules.yaml` beside its own module — outside the repo's root (an editable install launched elsewhere) the configuration listed no rule, the audit none, and approving a proposed rule answered « detection_rules.yaml not found ». It now finds it as the `glorfindel` command does (H0, run from `/app`, was not affected). Approving a rule says where it went: the client's layer (P1), never the base — added, replaced or already there, and whether it is bound to a VM. A campaign's facts show each run's target VM (D-2). Mutant M409.
- **No test writes in the repository's folder, and the suite fails if one does** (HY1, 27/10, found by IA1's review). `test_store_cycle_never_notifies_a_no_op` called `store_cycle`, which writes `runs/<run_id>_debug.jsonl` relative to the current folder: run alone, it left `runs/run001_debug.jsonl` at the root of the repository or of a worktree. The test now runs in its `tmp_path`, and a session guard in `tests/unit/conftest.py`, armed when the conftest is imported (collection included), fails the run:
  - the writes of the test process under the repository, caches aside, named with the test that made them — this holds where `runs/` already exists, on an operator's checkout. Python's audit hook sees a file opened for writing, a folder made or removed, a file removed, renamed or replaced, a link made, `Path.touch`/`os.utime`/`os.truncate`, `shutil.rmtree` and `sqlite3.connect`. It does not see what a C library writes once a file is open, ChromaDB 1.x's store (written by its Rust core), a path relative to a `dir_fd`, or a subprocess's writes below the root;
  - at the root, whatever wrote it: a new `runs/`, or any new entry git does not ignore.

  Known limits (`docs/operations.md`): another process creating an entry at the root of the main checkout fails the run wrongly; a `TMPDIR` or `--basetemp` inside the repository; `os.makedirs(exist_ok=True)` on an existing folder counts; only `tests/unit/` is guarded. `tests/unit/test_repo_hermetic.py` measures the guard in a throwaway repository. The watch is unchanged. Mutants M910–M914, M950–M952.
- **The quarantine locks' test no longer depends on the order of the files** (HY1). `actions._QUARANTINE_LOCKS` is global: a test of another resource group's quarantine NSG (same name) left its lock, and the check read it. It failed under the harness's load and in reverse order, and `scripts/component_check.py` kept the alphabetical order to avoid it. A fixture now gives the test empty locks. It proves more than before: one key and one lock object for the id and the name, and they exclude each other. A new test checks that two resource groups' quarantines of one name are two locks. Passes alone, in order and reversed. Mutant M915.
- **The injectors' image installs from a lock, with litellm** (HY1). `annatar/Dockerfile` (Annatar's and Gothmog's image, the one `make orodruin-push` sends to Orodruin) installed `.[annatar]` unlocked, and the `annatar` extra did not declare `litellm`: `annatar campaign plan --llm` failed in the image. `litellm==1.86.1` (Glorfindel's pin) joins the extra, with `jsonschema` and `requests`, which the shared core imports (they came only through litellm and azure-core). `requirements/annatar.txt` is generated by `make lock` like `requirements/glorfindel.txt` (constrained by `requirements/dev.txt`); the image installs it with `--require-hashes`, then the project with `--no-deps --no-build-isolation`. `tests/unit/test_dependency_lock.py` covers it. The image on Orodruin must be rebuilt (`make orodruin-push`). Mutants M916–M917.
- **A block the reassertion could not put back is said, even if Glorfindel dies right after** (L1F, #229, 27/10, found by the invariant machine — L1): when re-applying a vanished block failed (the role removed, a lock, a policy), `glorfindel/reassert.py` wrote « already alerted » (`drift_alerted_at`) on the block *before* recording the card. A crash, or a failed card write, in between left the marker without the card, and every later pass returned on that marker: the block gone from Azure, the attacker back in, and nothing said until the block was in place again. The card is recorded first now, then the marker — at worst a card said again at each pass while the marker cannot be written, never silence on a block that could not be put back. The same order for an isolation that could not be put back. Sequence frozen in `tests/unit/test_invariant_regressions.py`, unit tests in `tests/unit/test_reassert.py`; mutants M900–M902. Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`.
- **A reset no longer removes another VM's older isolation from an NSG it shares** (I4F, #235, 27/10, found by the invariants at 2 000 sequences, seed 101). An older version's isolation of a VM — its fixed names (`glorfindel-isolation-deny-all`, any/any), its state lost — stays on that VM's own NSG. A new VM is then attached to the same NSG and isolated by quarantine, so its NIC moves away. `glorfindel reset --from-azure` on the new VM read its original NSG to sweep what an earlier fallback left there. It counted only the NICs attached right now: one, the other VM's. It read the NSG as the new VM's alone, took the fixed names for its own, and deleted the other VM's isolation, which reopened that VM. The same held for a NIC left bare. The NIC away now counts among its NSG's NICs, so the NSG reads as shared. The fixed names are no longer the new VM's to take, and the reset says it cannot attribute them (`swept_partial`) instead of deleting them. The release was not affected: it puts the NIC back before reading. Regressions in `test_invariant_regressions.py`, mutants M960–M961.

### Security
- **A card's detail no longer runs what Azure or the model wrote** (#248, 27/10, found by the 5.5 review of the console's cards): the detail of a card wrote its reason, its suggested steps and its proposed query raw into the page. An Entra ID application's display name (any user of a tenant may register one), an NSG rule's name or the model's text such as `<img src=x onerror=…>` ran in the operator's console — and could call its routes (approve a held isolation, change a mode); the Content-Security-Policy of #222 has no `script-src`, so it did not stop it. The three are escaped now. Mutants M576–M578; M579 holds the anchor VM's Restore off the page (the server does not refuse it).
- **The hooks that refuse a real infrastructure identifier hold in every worktree and every clone** (27/10, found by the CI/CD session). `.githooks/pre-commit` and `commit-msg` read their denylist from `--git-dir` — in a worktree `.git/worktrees/<name>/`, where the list is not: they ran and let everything through, silently, for every commit made from a worktree (all of elrond's lots). They read it from git's common directory now. And they were stored non-executable (100644): git ignored them in any fresh clone. Stored 100755 now. Nothing leaked: every change merged on 27/10 and the whole tree were scanned against the list. Tests: `tests/unit/test_githooks.py` (a throwaway repository and worktree, `core.hooksPath` relative and absolute); mutants M903, M904.
- **The Makefile passes every secret by its name** (L23, #228, HY1). `docker run -e AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET=$(…)` (`annatar-run`, `annatar-dry-run`, `annatar-shell`, the `glorfindel-*` targets) showed the value in `ps`, the shell's history and `make -n`. The recipe's shell now chooses the value (`ANNATAR_` / `GLORFINDEL_AZURE_CLIENT_*` first, then `AZURE_CLIENT_*`) and sets it in `docker run`'s environment only; the command line carries `-e AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET`, a name. The canary, `tests/unit/test_makefile_secrets.py`, renders every target with `make -n` and fake values for every secret the Makefile names, then runs the containers' recipes against a fake `docker`. A value the shell expands (`-e NAME="$NAME"`) never reaches `make -n`'s text yet shows in `ps`: the rendered text also refuses `-e NAME=`, `--env NAME=` and, on a line that runs ssh, `export NAME=`, for every secret's name. Mutants M918–M919, M953–M954.
- **The console carries a Content-Security-Policy** (#222, 27/10): `frame-ancestors 'none'` (never framed by another page — Approve & execute under a decoy), `connect-src 'self'` (never talking to another origin, the live feed's WebSocket included), `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'self'` — on every response, the token page's included. No `script-src`: the Glorfindel page's inline handlers would break. Mutant M568.

## [0.3.1] — 2026-10-27 — "the defender, hosted and pinned"

### Added
- **Annatar and Gothmog do not act without Glorfindel** (V1, #214, 27/10). Before each run — each run of a campaign too — and before any call to the cloud, `annatar run`, `annatar campaign run`, `gothmog run` and `gothmog preflight` read the watch's heartbeat (`eregion_core/watch_gate.py`): the return channel when `EREGION_WATCH_CHANNEL` is set, else the local `~/.glorfindel/watch_heartbeat`; one younger than 90 s is a live watch. None: the run is refused, exits 1, and says how old the last heartbeat is (a refusal names where it read), or « never » — no contract armed, no target read, no window opened. A campaign with no watch is refused whole and stays `ratified`; a watch that dies mid-campaign stops it at the next run (`aborted`). Annatar's warning « no active glorfindel watch detected » is gone with it; its wait for a detection reads the heartbeat through the same code. `--without-glorfindel` runs anyway, for development (Orodruin's Make targets: `WITHOUT_GLORFINDEL=1`): the report says so (`metrics.without_glorfindel: true`, `metrics.detection_note` « detection not measured, by choice »), the detection is not waited for and no `detection_missed` is emitted; H0 accepts that report (`run_report_refusal`, `schemas/run_report.schema.json`), and refuses one that would claim `detected` or `missed`; the console shows « not measured, by choice » apart from `unknown`. A heartbeat dated more than 10 s ahead is no watch (`watch_channel.recent`, the wait's test too). A campaign run `--without-glorfindel` carries it in its manifest (`without_glorfindel: true`, `schemas/campaign_manifest.schema.json`), resumes only the same way, has no analysis report (IA0) — on Orodruin nor on H0 — and never enters the index nor a comparison (D-2): « campaign without Glorfindel: no detection report, by choice »; the console counts it apart. A campaign refused for no watch says it in one line; an `aborted` campaign exits 1. `WITHOUT_GLORFINDEL` takes only `1`, `yes` or `true`. `annatar clean` (preparation, not an attack) reads no watch. A `--dry-run` needs no watch. Tests: `tests/unit/test_watch_gate.py` (a cloud that fails if it is called), `tests/unit/test_gothmog.py`; mutants M720–M739, M750–M759. Docs: `docs/operations.md`, `docs/design/console.md` §2, `gothmog/README.md`.
- **A simulated AWS world, an AWS adapter written against it, and the invariants on AWS** (MC1, #92, 26/10, code only: no AWS call, no account, no new dependency). `tests/unit/fake_aws.py` is the AWS counterpart of the fake Azure: EC2 and VPC in memory, with AWS's semantics as its documentation states them — security groups that only allow, stateful, 1 to 5 per ENI; network ACLs, stateless, numbered, allow and deny; Amazon's DNS and IMDS filtered by neither; errors in botocore's `ClientError` shape. Each is a hypothesis (H1–H14), to be measured on an AWS bench (MC2): `docs/design/monde-aws.md`. `glorfindel/providers/aws/` (`AwsConnector`) talks to an injected EC2 client with boto3's names and answers, and imports no SDK. It isolates by a quarantine security group per VPC in place of each ENI's groups (the original recorded in state and as a tag), with no fallback by rules — a security group can't deny and an ACL would cut the whole subnet, so with the quarantine off or the configuration unreadable it refuses before any write. It blocks with deny entries in the subnet's network ACL — reaching every subnet associated with that ACL, often the whole VPC for its default ACL, and said (`scope: network_acl`, `subnets_affected` read in the ACL's associations) — recorded before they are created (an ACL entry has no name), placed ahead of the customer's allows, read back, and removed only while they say what Glorfindel wrote and no other block holds them. It refuses an ARN of another account or region, and everything when it has no account or region configured (I12); a group named like its quarantine that lost its `managed-by` tag is never released over; and declares `network_quarantine` and `network_rules` (blocks only) — no `session_drain`, no `restore_point`. `tests/unit/test_invariants_aws.py` runs the invariant machine on it, with the shared harness of `test_invariants.py` (faults, two operations at once, the real locks) and a world that moves the AWS way (`terraform apply` puts an ENI's groups back or strips an ACL, someone takes an ENI out of the quarantine onto `default`); I1–I12 and L1 hold, transposed where AWS differs (I4: every block reaches every subnet of its ACL, and another instance's block still holds; I10: no fallback; I12: account and region); U1 has no equivalent. Mutants M600–M623, and an AWS control set M630–M645 written before the machine and never used to shape it: 9–10 of 16 caught in every round at the Monday budget, Azure's 9–11 of 16, depending on the draw. `mutants.yml` gets 180 minutes. The Azure suite, its invariants and its harness are unchanged. Tests: also `tests/unit/test_aws_adapter.py`. Docs: `docs/design/monde-aws.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`, `docs/architecture.md`.
- **Rule layers: the shipped base stays intact, the client's rules live in their own layer** (P1, #86, 26/10). `proposed_rules.approve` wrote an approved rule into `glorfindel/rules/azure/detection_rules.yaml`, the file shipped with Glorfindel: an update replaced it and the rule was lost, and on H0 the modified checkout stopped the deployment (M-3). Now the rule goes to the client's layer, `~/.glorfindel/client_rules.yaml`, outside the repository (same `approve` signature, so the War Room's « Approve » is unchanged), and `load_config` loads it over the base for every caller. A client rule with a base rule's name replaces it; `disable: [<name>]` (or the client rule `enabled: false`) disables a base rule; each is said — in the watch's banner, logged once per process, and in `glorfindel rules`, which lists every rule with its layer. An unreadable layer leaves the base loading and is never written over; the rule is written before the proposal is marked approved. Exporting a rule generalised, without any identifier, to propose it upstream is left to a lot of P2 (a deterministic leak check, then a human review before anything is sent). One invalid entry of the client's layer (`interval_s: 30s`, `name: [x]`, a key that is no list) is skipped and said: the other rules and the base still load. An approved rule now binds to the backend that watches its workspace (`assets: [auto]`; the first rule's backend only when the proposal names no workspace — `glorfindel rules` shows `auto (<backend>)` or `auto (aucun backend : inerte)`): before, `rule_from_proposal` read a field `DetectionRule` lacks, and every approved rule took an inline form that applied to no discovered VM, silently — said now at load when it still happens. `glorfindel migrate-rules --from --base` moves rules approved into the base before P1 to the layer, binding such an inline rule to its backend (or moving it as is, and saying so); an unreadable file is said, nothing written. The containers mount the base read-only (`docker-compose.yml`, `Makefile`); `infra/glorfindel-host/deploy.sh` refuses a rules file modified on the VM and points to the migration procedure in `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, which `tests/unit/test_host_deploy.py` replays as written on a local repository. Tests: `tests/unit/test_rule_layers.py`; mutants M670–M678, M685–M688, M695–M699, M713–M716 (M528 follows `deploy.sh`). Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, `docs/operations.md`.
- **The Eregion console** (UI-1, #205, 26/10): `eregion console` (`pip install eregion[console]`) serves one console in place of the War Room and the atelier — an **Overview** (`/`: the cards to judge, the share of isolations and blocks judged wrong, each injector's last run and campaign, the channels), then a page per agent: **Glorfindel** (`/glorfindel`, the former War Room: TO JUDGE, the live feed, the infrastructure map), **Annatar** and **Gothmog** (`/annatar`, `/gothmog`: what each ran and what Glorfindel made of it, read only), and **Config** (`/config`, now an address). Glorfindel is always there; an injector is active once the host holds one of its runs (a window, a report, a campaign), else its tab is greyed and its page says what it would add — `eregion console --sections annatar,gothmog` (or `EREGION_CONSOLE_SECTIONS`) chooses instead, never without Glorfindel (`GET /api/sections`). The code moved to the `eregion_console/` package; its pages are package data, so a non-editable install serves them. Mutants M400–M407.
- **The design of the Eregion console** (26/10, #205, #206): `docs/design/console.md`. One console, `eregion console`, with a section per agent (Overview, Glorfindel, Annatar, Gothmog, Config) in place of the War Room and the atelier. Glorfindel is always present — it is the product — and Annatar and Gothmog require it: before each run they will check that Glorfindel's watch is beating (`--without-glorfindel` for a development run, written in the report). Old URLs, the `glorfindel war-room` command and `GLORFINDEL_WARROOM_TOKEN` keep working through aliases.
- **No provider SDK outside its adapter, checked in CI** (A1, MC0, 26/10): `tests/unit/test_provider_boundary.py`, also the « Provider boundary » step of `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. In `glorfindel/`, an `azure`, `boto3` or `google-cloud` import outside `glorfindel/providers/<provider>/` fails; elsewhere, outside its component's adapter file. Three credential reads of the shared core (`eregion_core/inscription.py`, `exercise_channel.py`, `orodruin.py`) are listed as known debt, a list that can only shrink. Mutants M450–M463. On `glorfindel.actions`, patch with `monkeypatch.setattr`, not `mock.patch` (it deletes on exit: the façade refuses, naming what to use).
- **The campaign report says what changed since the previous campaign, computed by the code** (D-2, #184, 26/10, code side). The first report written on H0 was faithful but learned nothing: its index was empty, and the one novelty (T1110.001 isolated at 0.78 on 23/10, blocked at 0.80 on 21/10) was found by hand — a similarity search over earlier runs does not guarantee to face the same technique on the same target. Now the previous campaign is chosen by a rule, never by the index's similarity: the latest `done` campaign of the index that started before this one and shares at least one key — a technique played on a target VM, the VM without case (`analysis/campaign_report.py`, `previous_campaign`); an aborted campaign, a later one, or one without a shared key is never it, and when there is none the section says why. The executed runs of both are joined exactly by key (the n-th run of a key faces the n-th) into typed facts, `ChangeFact` (`analysis/campaign_changes.py`): detection, latency, action, confidence and escalation, before and after, with the latency and confidence differences; a key on one side only is listed as such; an unknown decision stays unknown. The section « Ce qui a changé depuis la campagne précédente » is rendered by the code; the model gets the facts and may only cite them, in a new claim kind, `change`. `verify` checks them like the others: a `change` claim must cite a change fact, and every number a `change` or a `trend` claim writes, or an `observation` citing a change fact, must be one of its cited facts' — a latency, a confidence, a difference, of a change fact's two sides or of two cited runs, or a relative change in percent; a number written with a unit is of its unit's kind, a duration converted to seconds (« 2 minutes » is 120 s, « vue en 2 s » is not a 2-point difference) (technique and run ids, dates in figures, in words (in French or English) or as a range of days 1 to 31 after « le », « entre », « du », « des », « aux »… (never « de »), years, hours, ordinals and VM names are not read as numbers; a count of what is counted, « les 2 campagnes », is allowed up to the runs cited — « 1 heure » is no count). A `change` or `trend` claim, and an `observation` citing a change fact, name only their facts' actions and detections, and « de X à Y » / « X → Y » must go the way the fact went, with or without a unit, and from one side of a fact to its other side (« de 106,9 à 91,9 », « de 0,78 à 0,8 », « de 91,9 s à 15 s » or « de l'isolement au blocage » is removed); a trend's runs of this campaign face its earlier runs of the same technique, and two of its earlier runs of the same technique face each other both ways — their order is not known — so a true trend over three campaigns (« de 120 s (19/10) à 91,9 s (21/10), puis à 106,9 s ») is kept; a way inverted between an earlier run and one of this campaign stays removed, unless another cited earlier run holds the same action, or the same value, as the run of this campaign: the trend may then describe the step between two earlier runs, whose order is not read (known limit; reading that order, from the campaign's timestamped id or `campaign_started`, is left for a later lot). The computed section is what counts: « plus tard », the escalation, dates in the wrong order, a bare number that happens to be a fact's and numbers in words are not read — docs/analysis.md, known limits. « a isolé » on an unknown decision passes only through a change fact whose other side's decision is known (#167). A run without a run id is on its side of the comparison. Each run of the index now carries its campaign's state and start and its VM (`RunFact.target`); rows indexed before are never chosen. `python -m analysis.campaign_index --all runs/campaigns` adds them, idempotently, keeping the facts a report already indexed (a window is emptied a week after its end: computed again, a decision would be unknown); on H0, Jonathan runs it in the watch's container, and the 21/10 campaign, which never went through H0, must be copied there first, before its windows are purged. Tests: `tests/unit/test_campaign_changes.py`, one end to end in `test_hosted_report.py`; mutants M260–M279 and, after the four reviews, M510–M519 and M530–M547. Known limit, for a later lot: the start of a campaign that came through the channel is read from its manifest, unchecked by H-1, so a wrong date can name the wrong « previous » campaign (its facts stay the true indexed ones). Docs: `docs/analysis.md`, `docs/design/preuves.md`.
- **The atelier console: Annatar and Gothmog seen from the War Room** (26/10, read only). A second page, `/atelier` (« ⚒ Atelier » in the War Room's header, behind the same token), shows what the injectors ran and what Glorfindel made of it, from the files that reach the War Room's host. Exercises: each window of `runs/exercises/` with its state (open, in grace, closed, or lapsed — never closed, its injector died), the watch's cycles on a target inside it (the return channel's rule, `covering_runs`), the cards carrying its run, and the run's report when it is on the host; a technique expects a detection of its own technique (in a campaign, the next run on the same VM falls in the grace), a fault nothing disruptive, an unattributed detection does not count, and an absence in the host's files is never shown as a miss — only the run's own verdict says `missed`. Campaigns: the manifests of `runs/campaigns/`, and on demand the campaign report's facts (Glorfindel's decision on each run, or why it is unknown) and its `report.md`. Channels: the watch's local heartbeat, the newest window received, and the heartbeat the watch publishes on the return channel — the one a run on Orodruin reads — with the live windows it does not list yet; the detections delivered to a run. Catalogue: techniques and faults, with the last window of each. Every route is a GET (`/api/atelier/{catalog,exercises,campaigns[/<id>],channel,channel/detections/<run_id>}`): a run starts from an armed Orodruin, and a launch button, later, goes through that contract. The War Room never pulls a channel nor writes a window (the watch may purge one, H-1); a campaign's detail says which scenarios its facts leave out, and why. The report of a single Annatar run and of a Gothmog run reach H0 with #193 (below). Tests: `tests/unit/test_atelier_api.py`. Docs: `docs/glorfindel.md`, « Atelier ».
- **The report of a run launched alone reaches H0** (#193, 26/10, code side): on the hosted console, a single `annatar run` and a `gothmog run` from Orodruin showed their window and what Glorfindel did, not their verdict — only a campaign published its runs' reports. Now, with `EREGION_EXERCISE_CHANNEL`, both injectors publish `runs/<run_id>.json` to the `exercises` container at the end of the run, right after its window closes (the report's last save, an atomic whose command raised included): the report only, the same container and assignment as the windows — the contract is unchanged, and the prefix keeps it out of the windows' pull. A failed publication is said (« run report … not published »), never raised. A campaign is unchanged: its runs go with it, under `campaigns/<id>/`. On H0, each pass of the `campaign-report` thread pulls them into `runs/<run_id>.json`, where the console already looks (`exercise_channel.pull_runs`): a plain name only, and a report that passes `run_report_refusal` (H-1) — a refused one is not written, and why is said once; never over a report H0 did not pull itself (a marker per run in `runs/.pulled-runs/`). Gothmog's report already held the probes' values before the fault (`metrics.before`, the same form as `metrics.after`, by VM): now tested; `seen_down_at` stays the only reading during the fault. The atelier shows both in a fault's window: each probe by VM, its expectation, its value before the fault and after it, held or not — the heartbeat after as its offset from the fault's end; the War Room keeps only that shape (`{name, ok, observed, expect}`, `ok` true only when it says `true`: the pull checks neither). Tests: `tests/unit/test_run_channel.py` (down to the console showing a pulled run's verdict), `tests/unit/test_gothmog.py`, `tests/unit/test_atelier_api.py`; mutants M240–M246. Measured on the bench on 26/10 (a run alone and a Gothmog run shown by H0's console); the probes' display not seen on H0 yet.
- **A disruptive action runs alone only when several draws of the decision agree** (D-1, #186, 26/10). The same SSH brute force drew `block_suspicious_ip` at 0.80 on 21/10, then `isolate_vm` at 0.78 on 23/10. No temperature was set, so nobody could tell context from draw. Now, when `isolate_vm` or `block_suspicious_ip` is about to run on its own, the model is asked again (`GLORFINDEL_DECISION_DRAWS`, 3 draws in all by default), with the same messages and at the model's default sampling. The action runs only if every draw would have taken it on its own: same action, no escalation, above the confidence threshold. Otherwise the action is held on a new `unstable_decision` card, which can be approved in one click and lists the other draws. A draw whose call fails counts as a disagreement. A malformed value, 1, or more than 10 holds every disruptive autonomous action. A `human_only` VM, or an action another gate already held, pays for no extra draw. Each draw is kept in the cycle's debug line (`decision_draws`). Measured on 26/10 on `claude-sonnet-5-5`: alone, the brute force drew a block in 15 draws out of 15; after root on the same VM, 8 blocks and 7 isolations out of 15. All 5 runs of that case were held, so the 21/10–23/10 swing was the draw. The 4 clear threats ran 12 times out of 12, with no disagreement.
- **The harness publishes what each case drew** (D-1). `scripts/llm_smoke.py` prints each case's actions and confidences over `--runs` (min–max, median), and the gate that held them. It also prints the sampling actually used, how many cases drew a single action, and a pair of cases written in advance: the same brute force alone (expected `block_suspicious_ip`), then after a `sudo` to root on the same VM (expected `isolate_vm`), with a verdict on whether the context moves the decision. `--json` keeps every draw.

### Changed
- **The console on the host: its token under both names, from the operator's machine** (UI-1c, #205, 27/10). `make glorfindel-host-url` and `-tunnel` print « Console → … » and read the token from `host.env` with the console's own rule — `EREGION_CONSOLE_TOKEN` when it is not empty, else its former name, `GLORFINDEL_WARROOM_TOKEN` — the way compose reads the file (the last line of a key wins; `# …` after the value and a blank is a comment); without either, `-url` says so instead of printing an URL without a token. `make glorfindel-host-status` (`infra/glorfindel-host/status.sh`) reads it on the VM under either name, never prints it nor puts it on curl's command line (the header goes through curl's stdin), names the console's service (« console  eregion-war-room »: the compose service keeps the War Room's name until UI-1d), and says « jeton refusé » or « aucun jeton » instead of « pas de réponse ». A new host's `host.env` (`make glorfindel-host-env`) gets `EREGION_CONSOLE_TOKEN`; H0's, under the former name, stays valid — nothing to rename. `make glorfindel-host-push` first runs `host_env.py --check`: it says which name carries the token, never its value, and refuses a `host.env` without one, or whose token is a comment (`KEY=   # note` is « # note » for compose, measured with `docker compose config`). `infra/glorfindel-host/host.env.example` wrote its notes that way: copied as is, a webhook URL or a Discord id left empty took the note as its value — every note is now above its line, and a test reads the example with compose's rule (only `GLORFINDEL_LLM_MODEL` and `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY` set, no value starting with `#`). The Makefile's other « War Room → » lines say « Console → ». Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_console.py` (the recipes run against fake `ssh`, `az`, `docker`, `curl`), `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py`; mutants M740–M749. Docs: `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md` (« La console d'Eregion », and what the next redeployment of H0 changes), `docs/operations.md`.
- **The console's header, the same on every page** (#205, 26/10, after a first look on H0): ⚙ Config sits on the right, where the War Room had it; the live feed's dot, which came and went with the page, moved to the LIVE FEED column it describes; the beats say their age (« watch · 35s ago », « return channel · 33s ago »).
- **The War Room's and the atelier's names and addresses** (UI-1, #205, 26/10), kept working: `/atelier` → `/`, `/api/atelier/*` → `/api/exercises`, `/api/campaigns`, `/api/catalog`, `/api/channel` (308, until 1.0; `/api/exercises` takes `injector=`, `/api/catalog` takes `kind=`); `/runs/<id>` opens a run in its injector's page; `glorfindel war-room` is a hidden alias of `eregion console`; the token is `EREGION_CONSOLE_TOKEN` when it is not empty, else `GLORFINDEL_WARROOM_TOKEN` (H0's host.env needs no change; compose passes both, empty when unset — read as merely set, the new one would have dropped the token there). One header for every page: one watch beat, the return channel when an injector is active, one clock, in UTC like every time shown (the atelier showed local time without saying so). A decision card is judged in TO JUDGE only: the VM card keeps Approve & execute, Ack and Cmd, and leads there; an exercise card links to its run, and the run's cards link back (`/glorfindel?card=<id>`). « Read-only » now only means the Azure rights. The windows' filter stops at 7 days, their keeping time. A window shows its VM's name, the full id on hover (it carries the subscription). Pages and scripts revalidate on each load (`Cache-Control: no-cache`). The token page is in English.
- **`glorfindel/actions.py` split by concern, behaviour unchanged** (A1, #71, 26/10). Its 4 394 lines moved, text unchanged (`scripts/check_paragraphs.py` finds every one), into `glorfindel/response/` — the provider-neutral core: autonomous actions, errors, resource ids, local state, locks — and `glorfindel/providers/azure/`, the Azure adapter: `AzureConnector` assembled from one mixin per concern (NSG rules read by content, NICs and NSGs, quarantine, isolation, release, verification, blocks, unblocks, backup, restore, reset, read-only checks). No module is over 430 lines. `from glorfindel.actions import X` still works for every name, and the façade is still one namespace: a name patched through it (the tests' `_ISOLATION_STATE_DIR`, `_vm_lock`…) is patched in every module that reads it. The mutants that targeted `actions.py` follow their code (none stale); the full suite, the invariants and the mutation harness, control set included, hold as before.
- **The provider interface (MC0)**: each provider declares its capabilities — `network_quarantine`, `network_rules`, `session_drain`, `restore_point`, each with the rights it needs (`glorfindel/providers/interface.py`, `glorfindel/providers/azure/capabilities.py`). The readiness check reads the rights from that declaration instead of its own Azure names (same verdicts and messages on Azure); a test fails if the declaration drifts from what `check_permissions` asks Azure. `AzureMonitorDetector` moved into the adapter (`glorfindel.detectors` re-exports it), and a restore job's status is read through `CloudConnector.restore_job_status` (`glorfindel/jobs.py` imported the backup SDK).
- **The decision is asked at temperature 0 where the model accepts it** (D-1). `claude-sonnet-5-5`, the default model, refuses it (« `temperature` is deprecated for this model. », measured 26/10), so it is asked again without a temperature, once per process. Each cycle's debug line records the sampling it got: `decision_temperature` is `0.0`, or `null` for the provider's default. `claude-sonnet-4-6` and local models accept 0.

### Fixed
- **The console reaches the VM's managed identity again** (#205, 26/10, seen on H0 right after the deployment of UI-1): `eregion console` did not drop the empty `AZURE_CLIENT_ID` / `_SECRET` / `_TENANT_ID` that compose passes on a host without a service principal — the `glorfindel` command does —, so azure-identity stopped on an empty secret and every read of the console failed: no NSG, no vault, every exercise window « NOT ENROLLED », the return channel unreadable. The watch and the bot, started by `glorfindel`, were not affected. Mutant M408.
- **`orodruin-kill` stops what runs, whatever the operator's CLI context** (26/10, M-1, #181): `az role assignment list --all` only covered the CLI's current subscription — in the wrong context the kill said « no role assignment left » and exited 0 with every right intact. It now lists and deletes on the contract's subscription (`AZURE_SUBSCRIPTION_ID` of `orodruin.env`, `SUBSCRIPTION=` to force it) and on the VM's own when it differs, reads the listing again after the deletion, and says « nothing left » only from that listing — and, when nothing was there, only if the principal is the VM's own identity. Then it cancels the chaos experiments still running on the bench (they act with their own identity: the revocation does not stop them), deallocates the VM, closes the exercise windows still open in the channel (so the hosted watch stops labelling their targets as exercise after the grace, instead of up to 2 h 15), and says what is left in flight: a Run Command already sent, an atomic cleanup to replay, a VM a cancelled fault may leave down, the grace of a closed window. Any failed step: exit 1. The operator writes the windows: a new `channel_operator` assignment (Storage Blob Data Contributor on the `exercises` container only, for whoever applies `infra/orodruin`) — `make orodruin-lock YES=1`; until then the kill says the windows could not be closed. Nothing printed names the subscription. Tests: `tests/unit/test_orodruin_kill.py`, with a fake `az` whose current context is another subscription; mutants M191–M193, M66 updated. **Measured on the bench** (26/10, a kill during a `vm-shutdown` of gondolin): the 5 assignments deleted and the re-read listing empty, the experiment cancelled (`Cancelled` about 50 s later, the VM restarted by Chaos Studio itself), the window closed and pulled by H0 within 11 s, exit 0. After re-arming, `orodruin-check` is `armed` at once but Log Analytics still refused Gothmog's heartbeat query for a few minutes: `make gothmog-preflight` before the next fault. What the contract shows (`orodruin-check`, a passive run's reasons) no longer names the subscription — an unreadable listing printed its URL (M194). A VM a fault may leave down is said once in the kill's in-flight list.
- **The atelier reads enrollment as the watch does** (26/10, #185, after H-1): since H-1 a window marks a VM only if the reader finds it enrolled, a check the watch sets at startup — the War Room did not, so on H0 the atelier would have attached no cycle to any window and kept no run of a published campaign (the tests enroll every VM, which hid it). The War Room now sets the same check at startup (`TagEnrollment`, the host's identity), and the console shows `NOT BELIEVED` (with `window_refusal`'s reason) and `NOT ENROLLED`. A campaign's header no longer overflows its column. Test: `test_the_war_room_reads_enrollment_as_the_watch_does`.
- **The campaign report reads the order of the runs it compares, and checks a channel manifest's dates** (D2b, #184, 26/10, after D-2's reviews). A trend's cited runs of one technique now face each other in the order they were played — by `seq` within a campaign, else by their campaign's start as the index keeps it beside each run (`campaign_started`), else by a campaign id that is a date — so a pair of earlier runs is no longer an alibi for a way they never went (blocked on 19/10, isolated on 21/10 and 23/10: « de l'isolement au blocage » is now removed); both ways are still read between two earlier runs only when their order is unknown (rows indexed before D-2). A campaign that came through the channel has the start its manifest gives checked against what H0 saw: not after H0 pulled it (`.pulled`), not after the first window of its runs that H-1 believes, nor more than 1 h before it (5 min of clock skew); out of bounds, it is refused, said in the report and by `analysis.campaign_index`, and the window's start is taken — a forged date no longer changes the previous campaign. The last date phrasings: « au » and « the » alone no longer open a range (« tombe au 12, le 23 octobre » keeps 12 a number), while « au 21 et au 23 octobre », « From the 21 to the 23 October » and « On the 21st and 23rd October » are read as dates. A date no UTC date holds (« 9999-12-31T23:59:59-05:00 ») is refused and said as read, never raised; an unknown start is written into the index (empty), so that a start an earlier indexing gave does not stay. Tests: `tests/unit/test_campaign_changes.py`; mutants M650–M669, and M540–M542, M546, M547 follow the code (69 D-2 mutants caught, none stale).

### Security
- **An exercise window is no longer believed on its word** (H-1, #187, 26/10). Anyone who could write a file in `runs/exercises/`, or a blob in the exercise channel, could pass a real attack off as an exercise. The reader now refuses a window that lasts longer than 2 h, starts more than 5 min after it was written (a window written today for tomorrow stays refused tomorrow), or takes the RulePoller's run name (`watch-…`). A window marks a VM only if the watch itself reads the VM as enrolled, from its tags and its resource group's (Azure Resource Manager, cached 10 min). When those tags cannot be read, nothing is marked and every cycle is labelled real. Measured on the bench on 26/10 with the operator's credentials, and on H0 the same day through the VM's managed identity (`Reader` on the bench group): the bench target is enrolled, the VM carrying a real load is not. A run id marks only a VM its window names. A window is purged a week after its last moment; its tombstone keeps the channel from bringing it back. Exercise cards now stay in the War Room's TO JUDGE list, flagged, instead of being hidden.
- **H0 checks what a campaign brings through the channel** (H-1). Before the report is written, the manifest and each run's report are checked against their shape (`analysis/campaign_schema.py`, `schemas/campaign_manifest.schema.json`, `schemas/run_report.schema.json`). An executed run is kept only if its window is known and believed, names its VM, and that VM is enrolled; otherwise the report says why it was left out. The report also says which facts came through the channel and which H0 saw itself.
- **The hosted Glorfindel deploys a named reference, never a branch** (M-3, #183, 26/10, code side). `make glorfindel-host-start` ran `git pull --ff-only` on main and rebuilt: H0 ran whatever had just been merged. It now takes `REF=<tag or commit>` (by default the latest `v*` tag, in version order) and sends `infra/glorfindel-host/deploy.sh` over ssh: `git fetch --tags`, a detached checkout, then the image built with the reference as labels (`org.opencontainers.image.version` / `.revision`). A branch (`main`, `origin/main`, `HEAD`), a name that is neither a tag nor a commit, a `REF` that starts with `-` (it would be an option of the script), a `REF` taken from the environment rather than make's command line (a generic name, exported for something else), or a tracked file changed on the VM stops it before anything is built. `glorfindel/rules/`, which the containers write (approved rules), rides along — unless the reference changes the same file: the script then stops, names it, and points to the procedure (`infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Règles approuvées et nouvelle référence »), instead of saying « gardé » and letting git refuse the checkout. Make never expands the given `REF` (`$(value …)`, `unexport`), so a `$(…)` in it is refused, not run. `make glorfindel-host-status` reads the reference back from the running watch and warns when the checkout moved, sits on a branch, or the image carries none. Tests: `tests/unit/test_host_deploy.py` (local repositories, fake `ssh` and `docker`).
- **The dependencies are locked, with hashes** (M-3). `requirements/dev.txt` (CI, `make install`, invariants, mutants) and `requirements/glorfindel.txt` (the image), generated by `uv pip compile --universal --generate-hashes` (one file for Python 3.11 and 3.12, Linux and macOS; the second constrained by the first, so the image runs what CI tested), installed by pip in hash-checking mode, then the project alone (`--no-deps --no-build-isolation`; setuptools and wheel are locked too). The first lock is the resolution of 26/10; the suite passes on it in 3.11 and 3.12 and the image builds. It moves well ahead of what the workstations and CI ran (anthropic 0.104 → 2.3, fastapi 0.136 → 0.162, langgraph 1.2.1 → 1.2.17): the harness before H0's first redeploy on it is not optional. `make lock` regenerates it; an update goes through a PR and the harness (`make llm-compare`), run by Jonathan, before H0 is redeployed (`docs/operations.md`, « Verrou des dépendances »). `tests/unit/test_dependency_lock.py` fails when `pyproject.toml` asks for something the lock does not hold. The Annatar image is not locked yet.
- **Each container receives only its secrets** (M-3). `docker-compose.yml` passed one environment block, model keys, the bot's token and the War Room's token included, to the watch, the bot and the War Room. Now: the models' keys to the watch only, `DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN` to the bot only, `GLORFINDEL_WARROOM_TOKEN` to the War Room only, the webhook URL to the watch and the War Room, the Azure service principal and the settings without secrets to all three. `host.env` stays one file (compose fills each container's list from it). Since the watch and the War Room no longer see the bot's token, compose tells them only that it is set: `GLORFINDEL_DISCORD_BOT` (`${DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN:+1}`, `1` or empty, never the value). With it and `DISCORD_CHANNEL_ID`, the bot posts the cards and their webhook stays quiet (`escalations.discord_bot_configured`); a channel without a token runs no bot, so the webhook still posts and `glorfindel watch` still warns at startup when nothing alerts — an escalation always goes somewhere, or the watch says so. `host_env.py` copies the new variables. Tests: `tests/unit/test_glorfindel_host.py` (the compose, and each container rendered from a generated `host.env`).
- **The Discord buttons check who clicks** (M-3). `DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS` and `DISCORD_ALLOWED_ROLES` (Discord ids); anyone else gets an ephemeral refusal, logged by the bot (`Button refused: user …`), and nothing is judged, acknowledged or run. Closed by default: with neither set, every button refuses, and the bot says so at startup — the buttons run restores and Discord shows them to whoever reads the channel. Tests: `tests/unit/test_bot.py`, through discord.py's own dispatch, with a witness: the same restore click, allowed, runs to its end. H0 needs `DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS` in `host.env` before its next redeploy (`infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`, « Redéployer H0 après M-3 »). Mutants M280–M289 and M520–M528 (after review), all caught.

## [0.3.0] — 2026-10-23 — "the full loop runs"

The loop runs on the bench, end to end. From an armed Orodruin (O1), Annatar plays a pinned public attack (Atomic Red Team, R1) and Gothmog a declared fault (Chaos Studio, G0) on enrolled targets only (O0), and both verify the initial state after the fact. The hosted Glorfindel (H0) detects and decides in `human_only`, every exercise labelled as such (B2); the run records the detection and its real latency through the return channel (#157, #159). A campaign mixing the atomic with other techniques gets its report written by itself on the hosted Glorfindel, with Glorfindel's decisions and every claim checked against the runs it cites (IA0). Measured on the bench on 23/10: campaign `20261023T081120Z`, 4/4 techniques detected in 101.6 to 130.0 s, its report written once 14 s after publication, 4 decisions attached, 15 claims kept, none rejected. Still running: H0's four-week pre-observation (until about 13/11), then the review point (#70).

### Added
- **The report of a campaign launched from Orodruin writes itself on H0** (#167, IA0, code side, 21/10). Tests: `tests/unit/test_campaign_channel.py` and `tests/unit/test_hosted_report.py`, over an in-memory container (`tests/unit/fake_container.py`), a fake model and a fake index, including a local end-to-end run (published → pulled on H0 → `done` → `report.md` written once, the decision found in a `watch-*` file). Mutants M126–M149, M159–M169 and M180–M188, all caught. The campaign on the bench (the remaining "done when" of IA0, #69) comes after, with the Tests session, so none of this has been measured on the bench yet. With `EREGION_EXERCISE_CHANNEL`, `annatar campaign run` publishes the finished campaign to the exercise container (`eregion_core/exercise_channel.py`, `publish_campaign`). It publishes `campaigns/<id>/runs/<run_id>.json` for each run, then `campaigns/<id>/manifest.json`, and nothing else. It needs no new right: `REQUIRED_ACTIONS`, the `injector` role and the contract's scope are unchanged. The end-of-run message prints the published id, and announces the report on H0 only for a `done` campaign; otherwise it points to `make campaign-report`. A failed publication is reported, never raised. Windows are untouched: a campaign blob holds a `/`, which `_SAFE_NAME` refuses, and the window pull now pages through the listing. On H0, `glorfindel watch` starts a `campaign-report` thread (`analysis/hosted_report.py`), only with the channel set. Every 30 s it pulls the campaigns into `runs/campaigns/<id>/`: runs first, and a manifest only once all its runs have arrived. A campaign whose pull failed is not due at that pull. The thread writes `report.md` once, under a per-campaign lock, for a `done` campaign whose executed runs all have their report. A failure is noted in `report.error` and counted in memory, so an unwritable `report.error` cannot loop calls to the model. A report is tried at most 3 times; an `aborted` campaign gets no automatic report; no failure reaches the watch. Decisions come from the watch's `watch-*_debug.jsonl` when the run has no `runs/<run_id>_debug.jsonl`, by #157's rule (`first_detection`): same technique, same VM compared without case, seen between the run's finite `attack_time` and the end of its exercise window plus grace (`eregion_core/exercise.py`, `last_moment`, which `live` now uses as its bound). The index is `EREGION_ANALYSIS_INDEX`, else `~/.eregion/analysis/chroma`, mounted from the VM's disk into the watch (`docker-compose.yml`). The Glorfindel image now copies `analysis/`. The mount and the image take effect at the next H0 redeploy. New Make target: `glorfindel-host-campaign-report CAMPAIGN=<id>`. `glorfindel-start`, `-restart` and `-dev` create `~/.eregion/analysis`. Docs: `docs/analysis.md`, `docs/operations.md`, `docs/glorfindel.md`. A report written once now holds the decisions: an executed run, detected or of unknown detection (a silent return channel), whose decision is not yet in the watch's files keeps its campaign waiting, until the end of its window plus grace, or, without a readable window, until 15 min after the campaign's end — then 10 min more (`DECISION_LAG_S`), since the window bounds when the detection was seen, not when the cycle writes its decision: some 25 min after the campaign's end in practice. Without an `attack_time` or a `resource_id` nothing is waited for: the files are whole, nothing could ever be attached. No wait outlasts 2 h 25 after the manifest arrives. The wait and a refused run id are each logged once. Past it, the report is written and says, per run, why the decision is unknown (`fenêtre absente`, `attack_time absent`, `resource_id absent`, `décision non retrouvée dans la fenêtre`; each reads as an unknown, never as a nothing, since the model may copy it into a claim). The model gets `decision: null` with the reason. A found decision shows its action, confidence and escalation, in the table (`snapshot (40%, escaladé)`) and in the facts given to the model (`escalated`). On a run whose decision is unknown, the check rejects a `decision` claim by its kind alone; the rest reads the sentence, so it is a heuristic — a phrasing it does not know passes (`docs/analysis.md`): a trend or an observation that speaks of the response without saying the decision is unknown is rejected (one on the detection alone, « latence en hausse », is kept), and a claim of any kind that says Glorfindel did not act, or that it acted (isolated, blocked, snapshot, escalated, quarantined, contained, intervened, neutralised…), is rejected; the reasons given to the model count as « unknown » where a word begins, so a claim citing « décision non retrouvée dans la fenêtre » is not read as « did not act », while « Glorfindel n'a pris aucune décision dans la fenêtre » is rejected (it passed before the fifth review). Containment words (quarantaine, contenu, contain) also count as speaking of the response. A missed run takes no decision from the `watch-*` files; one found in its window (a late detection, or another run's) makes its decision unknown (`une décision dans la fenêtre d'un run manqué : détection tardive, ou d'un autre run`), never « did not act ». A failed pull of the windows puts the reports off to the next pull instead of writing them. The thread writes only campaigns pulled from the channel (a `.pulled` marker beside the manifest), never a local campaign of the same runs directory. A run id from the manifest that is not a plain name (`safe_part`) is never used as a file name: it is not awaited, it is reported, and its scenario is left out of the facts. On H0, an answer from the model without the tool call is a failed attempt (`build(..., strict=True)`), never an empty report written for good. The in-memory attempt count survives deleting `report.error` (restart the watch to try again). `campaign run --runs-dir X` warns when an executed run has no report to publish (the runs' reports stay in `runs/`), and then does not announce the report on H0. With `--no-report`, the campaign is not published either. A failed pull of the campaigns or the windows, a campaign not all pulled, or a report lock that cannot be opened, is logged once while it lasts (again if it comes back after a pull or an open that went through), not every 30 s; a failure is recognised by its type and HTTP status, not its text (a timed-out connection names an object `at 0x…`, new at every call); a `report.error` that cannot be removed after a report is written no longer escapes `write_report`. The call to the analysis model is bounded (sixth review): `EREGION_ANALYSIS_TIMEOUT_S`, 300 s an attempt (unreadable, zero, negative, infinite or above 3600 → 300; passed to the containers by `docker-compose.yml` and to the Annatar image by the Makefile). It went out with LiteLLM's own default, 6000 s an attempt, so a hung provider held the `campaign-report` thread, and every report after it, for hours without a word; it now times out as a failed attempt, counted, never reaching the watch. It is its own variable, not `GLORFINDEL_LLM_TIMEOUT_S`, which bounds only Glorfindel's calls: the report also runs where Glorfindel's package is not (the Annatar image), and writes up to 2048 tokens off any cycle's path. The Orodruin image must be rebuilt to write `attack_time` into each run's report (without it, no decision is attached), besides H0's redeploy.
- **Glorfindel fails closed when a dependency fails** (21/10, G2, #77): the model, the cloud API, the telemetry and the configuration, each proven by a full cycle of the graph (`tests/unit/test_dependency_failures.py`, with a positive control that does execute when nothing fails) and a mutant (M151–M158, M170–M178 after the review). **Model**: the decision call — and the rule-authoring call, which runs in the same VM queue — is bounded, `GLORFINDEL_LLM_TIMEOUT_S` (120 s an attempt; unreadable, zero, negative or above 3600 → 120): LiteLLM's own default is 6000 s an attempt, three with the retries, so a hung provider held the VM's decisions for hours with no card; it now ends as `cycle_failed`. **Cloud API**: a VM whose readiness check cannot reach Azure stays in `human_only`; an action that fails is `action_failed`, a read-back that fails is `verification_failed` — behaviour unchanged, now proven end to end. **Telemetry**: an investigative query that fails (workspace unreachable, `PartialError`, throttled) is no longer read as « no rows » — no backup agent, no reboot, no successful login, the absences that argue for isolating. What the model sees is unchanged (still `[]`, no end-to-end run needed); the failure is kept in the state (`telemetry_gaps`, in the debug line, never in the signal) and a disruptive action decided on it is held — new card type `telemetry_gap`, approvable in one click from the War Room; a snapshot stays autonomous; a card an earlier gate held (low confidence…) keeps its type and names the failed queries in its reason (M174). The model's confidence is bounded to [0, 1] once parsed (a 5.0 read 500 % on the card), and a `NaN` one is held by the confidence gate, written `not confidence >= threshold` (M172, M173). **Configuration**: an unreadable `glorfindel-config.yaml` runs every VM in `human_only`, `watch --mode` included (its per-VM rules — a VM pinned `human_only` — were lost with the file, and `--mode` made every VM autonomous); the card says the config is unreadable, the VM's configured mode unknown and the session's `--mode` ignored, and so does the watch's banner (it read `default = <--mode>`; M177, M178). Not yet: a card when the detection itself stays blind (the RulePoller's queries failing for good — the War Room's DETECT node turns red, nothing is posted). The real faults on the bench (Chaos Studio from Orodruin) are left to do with Jonathan: `gothmog/README.md`, « G2 ».
- **A campaign can play an atomic from the catalog** (#165, R1/IA0 code side): the T1136.001 catalog entry is now `engine: atomic`, with its `engine_ref` and its steady-state probes (taken from `account-creation-atomic.yaml`). The catalog schema accepts `steady_state` on a technique and requires it for an implemented atomic, like the experiment schema does for an experiment.
