The synthesizer writes an E1 experiment (no steps) for an atomic entry — the `engine_ref` resolved at plan time against the vendored atomics (`annatar/engines/atomic.py`, `resolve_ref`) — and the engine of the entry is authoritative: an atomic never falls back to a home-grown script, even with a `reference_script` lingering. An entry with no valid `engine_ref` or no probes is not synthesized. The campaign runner records the atomic run's detection and latency (#157) in the manifest, as for a single run. A Make target plans and runs a campaign from Orodruin (`orodruin-campaign-plan` / `orodruin-campaign-run`, on the model of `gothmog-run`); the inter-scenario `glorfindel reset` is not in the Orodruin image — its launcher is on PATH (every `[project.scripts]` entry is installed) but the `glorfindel` package is not copied — so the runner tests the package (`find_spec`) and then the launcher: when either is missing, the reset is skipped quietly, without shelling out, and noted on each scenario (`skipped: …`; `reset`; `failed: exit <code>` when an installed glorfindel exits non-zero). The plan does not depend on `jsonschema` or `schemas/`, which the Orodruin image lacks: it validates the experiment the way the run reads it (the scenario parser and the atomic engine's probe check), the E1 schema is checked by the tests, and no file is left behind when the validation fails or raises. The destructive guard (`--allow-destructive` and a `safe_target`) applies to every engine. Probes made of spaces are refused by both schemas. The plan target does not echo its `CAMPAIGN_TARGET` (it carries the subscription). **The campaign report does not write itself on Orodruin yet**: it needs `chromadb` and `litellm`, absent from the image (`[annatar]`), so the campaign says it was not written; bring the campaign back (`scp -r …:runs/campaigns/<id> runs/campaigns/`) and run `make campaign-report CAMPAIGN=<id>` locally (`docs/operations.md`). Glorfindel's blue fields are unchanged. Mutants M116–M125 (M117 also neutralizes the post-write check, so it measures the tests, not the self-check). The campaign on the bench (the remaining "done when" of IA0, #69) comes next, with the Tests session.

### Changed
- **The campaign report's table shows each run's detection latency** (#174, 23/10): a new « Latence » column, from the manifest's `detection_latency_s`, with one decimal (`106,3 s`), `—` without one. The facts given to the analysis model, its prompt and the check are unchanged. Test: `tests/unit/test_campaign_report.py`; mutant M190.
- **The bench, all or part** (23/10): `make celebrimbor-start` / `celebrimbor-stop` take `VM=<name>` (short or full name) for one VM, and print the VMs they act on first. Without `VM` nor `ALL=1`, `stop` spares the VMs tagged `always-on=true` — a new tag Celebrimbor sets on the VMs with `always_on: true` in `infra/terraform/config.yaml` (bree, the real load the hosted Glorfindel watches around the clock); `stop` used to deallocate bree too. Takes effect once Celebrimbor is applied (the plan: the tag added to bree in place, nothing replaced). Tested against a fake `az` (`tests/unit/test_makefile_bench.py`); mutant M189.
- **Decision: the rules fallback does not deny Azure DNS/IMDS, even on a NIC NSG that looks dedicated** (19/10, sixth review S7, last part). Azure only accepts these rules with source `*`, which reaches every NIC on the NSG, now or later; an NSG that looks dedicated at isolation time may not be (L24: a NIC away in quarantine, one attached meanwhile). The quarantine path denies them without that risk; the fallback keeps saying they stay reachable. Recorded in `docs/azure.md`, the ROADMAP and next to the code.
- **claude-sonnet-5-5 is the default model** (19/10, #139): after the harness (36/36, no overconfident error; claude-sonnet-4-6: 32/36, 4) and an end-to-end run on the bench (T1486 → isolate_vm 0.80, T1110.001 → block_suspicious_ip 0.88). One line in `eregion_core/models.py`. Annatar's campaign planner keeps claude-sonnet-4-6 (its own default, `DEFAULT_ANNATAR_MODEL`): claude-sonnet-5-5 declines the red-team planning prompt (`content_filter`, measured the same day) — the planner now says so instead of falling back in silence, and goes through the tool-choice fallback.
- **One definition of the default model** (19/10): it was written in eight places (compose, agent, War Room, four CLI options, Annatar's planner). `eregion_core/models.py` holds it; everything reads `glorfindel_model()` / `annatar_model()`, docker compose passes an empty variable for « default », and `tests/unit/test_models.py` fails on any other model name in the code. The default is still claude-sonnet-4-6; switching is one line after an end-to-end run.
- **The observation's Glorfindel decides with claude-sonnet-5-5** (19/10): in the harness, 36/36 good calls and no overconfident error (claude-sonnet-4-6: 32/36, 4), about three times faster, more cautious on confidence (12 needless holds, harmless in human-only). Switched on the hosted, read-only VM; the code default stays claude-sonnet-4-6 until an end-to-end run.
- Direction set for 1.0 (eighth pass): SOC functions for the cloud workloads of teams without a SOC, proven by attack and by failure. Three-agent loop — Annatar attacks, Gothmog breaks, Glorfindel detects and responds — with Orodruin as the disposable runner and Celebrimbor as the bench builder. Production rule: never a fault or a destructive action on a real workload. README, ROADMAP, `docs/architecture.md` (shared vocabulary, the three zones), session files for Orodruin and Gothmog, reserved folders `gothmog/` and `infra/orodruin/`.

### Fixed
- **A malformed environment switch never makes Glorfindel bolder** (21/10, G2): `GLORFINDEL_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` that can't be read, is outside [0, 1] or is `nan` now holds every autonomous action until fixed — it fell back to 0.7 (laxer than the 0.95 an operator mistyped « O.95 »), and `nan` parsed, so `confidence < nan` was never true and the gate let everything through (the War Room shows 100 %, no longer a NaN in its JSON). `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY` is read one way everywhere (`config.read_only_requested`): read-only unless unset, empty, or clearly off (`0`, `false`, `no`, `off`) — the connector accepted `1/true/yes` and the War Room `1/true/True`, so `on`, `TRUE` or `y` left the writes on. `GLORFINDEL_APPROVAL_MAX_AGE_H` at `nan` or `inf` is 2 h: `age > nan` was never true, a card of any age could be executed; at 0 or below it closes one-click approval, every card refused (as `age > 0` already did, now explicitly — a card dated ahead of the clock included). The threshold is read in `config.py` (`confidence_threshold`), so the War Room no longer imports the agent for it — an import that failed there dropped the whole capability matrix. `GLORFINDEL_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD`, `GLORFINDEL_LLM_TIMEOUT_S` and `GLORFINDEL_APPROVAL_MAX_AGE_H` now reach the containers (`docker-compose.yml`, `x-env`; empty is each reader's default): set in `.envrc`, `make glorfindel-start` ignored them. On the hosted VM (H0) they must be set by hand in `host.env`, which `host_env.py` does not fill (the defaults are the cautious ones, and H0 is read-only). Mutants M156–M158, M170–M171, M175–M176.
- **A card resolved while Discord had archived its thread is greyed again** (21/10, #169; seen on H0 on 20/10: `Sync d1dde0fd: … (error code: 50083): Thread is archived`, a card acknowledged in the War Room): Discord archives an idle thread without a gateway event, so the bot's cached thread still read open, the reopen of 19/10 was skipped and the edit refused — the message kept live buttons. On a 50083 the bot now reopens the thread and tries the edit again, once in the same attempt, then closes the thread as before; still one attempt per state change, a deleted message still forgotten. Mutant M150.
- **Mutant M57 measures again** (21/10): the check that a window labels only its targets had moved into `_covers` (`eregion_core/exercise.py`, O1 part C), so M57 no longer found its text and the harness left it out of the count without a word. It now drops the target check there, and three tests catch it on an assertion (`tests/unit/test_exercise.py`). The ROADMAP's lot table is whole again: the O1 row held bullet lines and a blank line, which cut the table short, so H0, R1, G0, B2 and IA0 showed as plain text.
- **Annatar no longer misses a RulePoller detection over a resource id's case, nor credits a run with an earlier detection** (20/10, #157; found by the first R1 bench run, checked on the closing one): the watch writes Log Analytics' `_ResourceId` in lower case, Annatar emits ARM's casing — `_check_watch_files` compared them as is, so every RulePoller detection was noted `missed` and a `detection_missed` made Glorfindel propose a duplicate rule. It also counted any detection of the same technique on the same VM from a file written within the last `timeout` seconds, even one that came before the attack. Both paths of the wait (the run's own `runs/<run_id>_debug.jsonl` and the `watch-*` files) now go through one pure rule, `annatar/runner/detection.py` (`detection_latency`): a `detection` of the run's technique, on its resource compared without case, seen at or after the run's `attack_time` — a detection of unknown time does not count. The return channel (#159) will reuse it on records read from storage. Mutants M98–M99.

- **The hosted Glorfindel no longer fills its disk** (20/10): each redeploy rebuilt the image and left the old layers in containerd (Docker 29) — 87 % of the 32 GB disk, a full disk would have stopped the watch and broken H0's four-week run. Pruned (back to 12 %); `glorfindel-host-start` now prunes dangling images after the build. `glorfindel-host-status` no longer counts warnings as errors (LiteLLM's missing-botocore notice made 4 false errors a day).
- **`annatar run` exits non-zero on a refusal** (20/10, #158; minor finding of R1's review): the engine returned its refusal in `RunOutcome.error` — Orodruin passive, target not enrolled, dirty start, an atomic the adapter will not run faithfully, cap, preflight, invalid scenario — and so did an error after the target was touched (no observable effect, initial state not restored or not verified), but the CLI ignored it and exited 0: a script or a CI chaining runs took it for a success. The CLI now exits 1 on any `RunOutcome.error`, with one line and a hint (`annatar/cli.py`, `run_exit_code`); 0 stays for a run that went through, a dry run, or an operator's « no ». `annatar campaign run` is unchanged (it marks that scenario in error and goes on). Mutants M103–M108.
- **A model that refuses a forced tool choice can still decide** (19/10): claude-sonnet-5-5 rejects `tool_choice` naming a tool (« type tool and any are not supported »), so every decision and rule proposal failed before the model saw the signal. `eregion_core/llm_call.py` retries once with `auto` and remembers the model; no tool call then → no decision → escalation (the defensive parse). The default model is called as before.
- **The ransomware rule no longer goes blind after a boot on a 60-s DCR** (#105, measured 19/10): the agent's first upload packs samples under one timestamp, `min(Gap)` read ~0 and forced fine mode for 10 min — on gondolin with a 60-s DCR, 74 and 99 MB/s samples were missed by the rule and seen by the fixed one on the same window. The step now ignores sub-second gaps (`minif(Gap, Gap >= 1s)`); no change on 3 days of benign data. Live, the hosted Glorfindel caught the write only once the batch left the window (07:09). Mutant M62.
- **litellm pinned to 1.86.1** (19/10): the dependency read `litellm>=1.0`; a rebuild picked 1.106.0, which imports an absent `mcp` module on the decision call — the hosted Glorfindel kept detecting but every decision failed (`cycle_failed`). `glorfindel-host-status` now counts the failed decisions of the last 24 h.
- **The bot's sync no longer loops on a failing edit** (19/10): Discord archives an idle thread (an edit there fails, 50083) and a message deleted in Discord is gone (10008) — both were retried every 15 s, 10 800 errors in a day. The thread is reopened for the edit and closed again, a deleted message is forgotten, and any other failure is tried once per state change.
- **Discord follows the War Room** (16/10): the bot only posted new cards — a card acknowledged or judged in the War Room kept live buttons in Discord, and repeats never showed. It now re-reads the cards it posted every 15 s and edits a changed message (a « État » field, buttons greyed once closed, « N× »), and archives a VM's thread once nothing is pending. The VM card's Right / Wrong buttons in the War Room also acknowledge, like the To judge list.
- **Discord buttons work after a restart** (16/10): the bot keeps each card's message id and gives it its view back at startup (cards of the last 14 days); every redeploy left them answering « interaction failed ».
- **`glorfindel audit <name>` finds the VM** (16/10): a name now resolves through the discovered VMs, for every command that takes one (it answered « not found in ? »); two VMs of that name → refused with both ids, never the first.
- **Tests no longer write the operator's discovery cache**: `AssetRegistry()` bound `~/.glorfindel/discovered_assets.json` at import, so the conftest's redirection did nothing (found when a new test wrote fake VMs there, 16/10 — cleaned).
- **The Discord bot no longer says « done » for what did not run** (16/10): the first real card read « IP suspecte bloquée » for a block human_only had held. The title says « Recommandé : … (non exécuté) » for a held decision; the embed carries the model's explanation, the IP:port, the rule and the confidence, then why nothing ran; Right / Wrong / Unsure buttons record the observation's verdict from Discord.
- **What the War Room got wrong once hosted** (16/10): « missing credentials » on a managed identity (the status now comes from what Azure answered); the autonomy mode editable in observe-only (locked to human-only, refused by the API); a « Run scenario » control posting to a route that never existed (removed, with `/api/scenarios`); drag-and-drop slots drawn as empty cards; every escalation in red in the feed (red now means isolate or block); each protected VM counted « unprotected » (the vault names items `VM;iaasvmcontainerv2;<rg>;<vm>`); the quarantine NSG on standby shown « restricted »; long rule names broken over five lines.
- **An empty AZURE_CLIENT_ID no longer hides the managed identity** (H0, measured on the hosted VM): docker compose passes `AZURE_CLIENT_ID=""` (and SECRET, TENANT) when the host has no service principal; azure-identity's `EnvironmentCredential` takes that for set, `ClientSecretCredential("")` raised, and `DefaultAzureCredential` never reached the VM's identity — discovery and posture failed. The CLI now unsets these variables when empty, before any command. Mutant M56.
- **An isolation by rules now says Azure's DNS and IMDS stay reachable** (sixth review, S7): it was in the outcome and shown nowhere. An orange « ⚠ DNS/IMDS » chip next to ISOLATED, a line in `glorfindel list`, a note in the autonomous isolation's notification. And the readiness gate learns of a quarantine swap refused at execution (an Azure Policy its rights read can't see): a `quarantine_refused` reserve, the isolation counted as by rules, until one puts the quarantine on every NIC. Mutants M52, M53.
- **A verification that raised mislabelled an isolation that did happen** (sixth review, S9): the exception left the graph, and the watch's safety net recorded a `cycle_failed` with an empty action and `executed: False` — no Revert button for a VM that was isolated. `verify_action` now treats it as an action that could not be verified: the action and its outcome are kept, and the card is a `verification_failed`, like an unreadable rule list. Mutant M51.
- **The session drain spared every session with a forensic source** (sixth review, S8) — any port, either direction — while the quarantine's allow only lets TCP in on the forensic ports: a session the VM opened towards that host, or one on another port, survived the isolation. The drain now spares only TCP sessions from a forensic source to a forensic port of the VM. The filter was checked with a real `ss`; mutant M50.
- **The account-creation rule (T1136.001) fires on real creations only** (seventh review, P1). It matched every line mentioning useradd: failures (« already exists »), the « new group » line of the same creation, package-manager system accounts. On 14 days of the bench it matched only Azure agents' own accounts. It now keeps « new user: name=… » lines, except a system account without a login shell and the known agent accounts without a shell — a system UID with a shell, or an agent's name with a shell, stays. Validated on the real KQL engine (9 cases; 0 line on 14 days instead of 6); same output columns.
- **The card's NSG group no longer contradicts the readiness gate** (seventh review, P2): « NSG precedence » failed (✗) as soon as an allow came before a Glorfindel deny, while the gate knows the quarantine NSG neutralises it for an isolation and makes it a reserve for a block. The audit now judges it with the gate's own rule (`readiness.isolation_by_quarantine`): ok when the isolation goes through the quarantine, a reserve (⚠) otherwise — never a failure, as in the gate.
- **The War Room's root dot no longer reports a breakdown for a fleet posture** (seventh review, P3): a VM without a usable backup turned it red, « A module is in error ». It is now orange and says how many VMs lack a backup; red stays for a module that actually fails (a rule's poll, the audit itself).
- **The audit read a 0-s cadence right after a boot** and claimed the ransomware rule's fine mode: the agent's first upload sends several samples under one timestamp (measured on the bench). The cadence is now the smallest gap between seconds that each carry one sample; until there is one, the audit says it can't tell yet. Measured: 10 s on the bench, the real cadence.

### Added
- **The real detection latency is recorded in the run** (20/10, #157, checked on the bench the same day): `runs/<run_id>.json` now carries `metrics.detection_latency_s` next to `detection`, on both engines (local-script and atomic), and `RunOutcome` passes it to the campaign manifest (read by the campaign report, IA0, and later the « time to contain » indicator). It is measured from the run's `attack_time` (T0, the command's call), not from `attack_started` (about a minute later on the atomic path, after the effect proof) nor from the start of the wait (after the cleanup): to the RulePoller's `timestamp`, or `attack_time + detection_time_s` for the watch's poll of the run's own `attack_started` (whose timestamp is the emission's, not the detection's). `null` when not detected. `annatar report <run_id>` shows it. Reference measured by hand on the bench run `20261020T121319Z`: 117.5 s. Checked on the bench run `20261020T143259Z`, written by the run itself through the return channel (#159): 44.7 s — 4.5 s to the `useradd` in the VM, 38.4 s of Log Analytics ingestion, 1.9 s to the RulePoller (Orodruin and the hosted Glorfindel both on the host's PTP clock, a few µs apart). Mutants M100–M102.
- **#159 — a run on Orodruin records what the hosted watch detected** (20/10, deployed and checked on the bench the same day): a `watch` blob container next to the exercise windows, managed identity only. The hosted Glorfindel writes a heartbeat (written at, started at, the exercise windows it knows) and its detections — only for a VM it attributed the detection to and that an open or in-grace window covers, never the rest of the fleet (`eregion_core/watch_channel.py`). The RulePoller notes each detection in memory; a thread delivers them and writes the heartbeat only once nothing waits, so a heartbeat vouches for what came before it. Annatar's wait reads the channel when no watch is local: its detections go through #157's rule (same technique, same VM without case, seen from the attack; latency from `attack_time`). With no detection, a miss needs a heartbeat written after the wait, by a watch up since before the attack, that knows the run's window — any channel failure gives `unknown`, with no `detection_missed`. Orodruin's contract accepts a read-only role on that container and nothing more: a writer role there leaves it passive, so an injector never writes what the watch says. `REQUIRED_ACTIONS` and the `injector` role are unchanged. Mutants M109–M115. Deployed with #163: docker compose did not pass `EREGION_WATCH_CHANNEL` to the hosted containers, so the watch would have published nothing; a test now holds every `host.env` variable reaching them. Checked on the bench run `20261020T143259Z` (R1, below): from Orodruin, with the watch on the hosted Glorfindel and no watch file on Orodruin, the run wrote `detection: detected` and its latency itself.
- **R1 — the first adapter toward a public attack library (Atomic Red Team)** (20/10, measured on the bench the same day): Annatar can run a *pinned* atomic instead of a home-grown script. The definitions are vendored under `annatar/atomics/` (only the techniques the catalog marks `implemented`; pinned to upstream commit `9f6a1eab`, 2026-02-18, MIT license kept beside them in `LICENSE.atomic-red-team`, and the sha256 of each vendored file recorded in `PIN` and checked by a test) and ship in the image — Orodruin has no Internet, so nothing is fetched at run time, and the upstream `invoke-atomicredteam` runner is not used. An experiment declares `engine: atomic` and `engine_ref: "<technique>/<auto_generated_guid>"` (schema E1, no steps); the adapter `annatar/engines/atomic.py` reads that test's own `command` and `cleanup_command`, substitutes the input-argument defaults, gives each an explicit `#!/bin/<executor>` shebang (Run Command's default shell is not the test's), and **fails closed** — it refuses a non-linux test, an executor other than sh/bash, declared dependencies, `elevation_required: false` (Run Command runs as root), a `#{…}` placeholder still present after substitution (so a `#{user-name}` never reaches the shell as a comment), and a command that points into `PathToAtomicsFolder` (the upstream tree is not on the VM). The runner (`annatar/runner/engine.py`) reuses the whole existing path — the Orodruin contract (every Run Command journaled and capped through `AzureVMExecutor.run_command`, new sibling of `run_script`), the inscription check, the exercise window (B2), and the `attack_started` / `detection_missed` feedback — and adds a verify-before / run / **effect-proof** / cleanup / verify-after cycle: `steady_state` probes on the VM confirm the initial state is clean before the attack (a dirty start is refused), that the command actually broke it (no observable effect → no `attack_started`, so Glorfindel is never asked to detect an attack that did not happen), and that it is restored after the atomic's own cleanup (not restored → the run is an error, with the resolved cleanup printed for the operator). The cleanup, after-probes and the report are in a `finally`, so they run even when the command raises (`attack: ERROR`, the exception re-raised for the campaign). An engine Annatar cannot run (a typo, or a future `stratus`/`chaos-studio`) is refused at authoring and by an explicit table in the runner — never a run with zero steps followed by a bogus `attack_started`. The detection result (`detected`/`missed`/`unknown`) is written into `runs/<run_id>.json` after the wait (#65's "done when"). First shipped experiment: `account-creation-atomic.yaml` (T1136.001, Syslog — telemetry Glorfindel already reads; the atomic creates a user with a login shell, so the post-#109 rule keeps it). Mutants M75–M97. Issue #65. Measured on the bench from an armed Orodruin, on gondolin, with the watch on the hosted Glorfindel (a single host was not feasible: Orodruin's container does not see the hosted watch, and a watch on Orodruin cannot reach Log Analytics). First run `20261020T121319Z`: PASS, but `detection: unknown` — the run could not see the watch — and a detection measured by hand (109.3 s); hence #157, #158 and #159. Closing run `20261020T143259Z`: PASS, `detection: detected`, `detection_latency_s` 44.7; five Run Commands, each journaled before its call (the Activity Log names Orodruin's identity as the caller); the cleanup's `userdel` seen in Syslog and the initial state verified by the after-probe; on the hosted Glorfindel, the card labelled `EXERCISE` (snapshot at 40 %, escalated for low confidence).
- **G0 — Gothmog runs a declared fault from an armed Orodruin** (19/10, measured on the bench the same day): `gothmog/` starts a Chaos Studio experiment (classic model; Workspaces are in public preview) declared in Celebrimbor — `infra/terraform/chaos.tf`, off by default: the VM enrolled with the engine (`Shutdown-1.0`), a graceful 5-minute shutdown, the experiment's identity with a custom role on that VM only (read, power off, start — not delete). A run arms the contract, reads the targets in the experiment itself and passes each through the inscription (`kind="fault"`), and injects only if every steady-state probe holds (VM running, guest agent ready, Heartbeat in Log Analytics — all through ARM, the one egress Orodruin leaves open). During the fault the VM must be seen down; past its duration and a margin the experiment is cancelled. After it, the return is verified by the probes (a heartbeat later than the fault's end), with a declared fallback (start the VM); the engine's « success » is never taken for a return. Each write (start, cancel, start the VM) is journaled before it is sent; the run is recorded like Annatar's (`runs/<run_id>.json`), inside an exercise window of kind `fault`. Its signals go to `runs/gothmog/`: Glorfindel's watch sends every event of `runs/*_signals.jsonl` but `attack_started` to its decision graph. Orodruin's contract and injector role gain the four Chaos Studio actions; its image ships Gothmog. `gothmog run|preflight|report`, `make gothmog-preflight|gothmog-run`. Mutants M71–M74. Measured: run `gothmog-20261019T200123Z` PASS — the start journaled 0.45 s before the execution began, gondolin seen down 32 s in, the engine brought it back after 5 min, the return verified 133 s after the fault's end (a later heartbeat), no fallback; the `fault` window reached the hosted watch's channel; Glorfindel did nothing on gondolin (no cycle, no escalation). Found on the way: `last` is a KQL keyword — the heartbeat probe failed closed (the run would have been refused, nothing injected); the column is `LastSeen`.
- **O1, code side — an injector acts only from an armed Orodruin** (19/10, not yet deployed): `eregion_core/orodruin.py` reads the contract without writing — the host's role assignments at every scope inside the bench's resource group, its effective rights there, a connection outside the perimeter that fails, a declared cap under its ceiling — and answers `armed` or `passive` (an unreadable contract is passive). Annatar's `run` and `clean` check it before reading the target; passive, nothing is touched. Every call that writes on a target goes through one point in the executor, journaled to disk before it runs, and stops at the cap. `orodruin-kill` revokes the identity's role assignments, then deallocates the VM; a failed revocation still stops it and exits non-zero. Terraform module `infra/orodruin/` (separate state, managed identity with a custom role limited to the injectors' actions on the bench, egress closed except Azure's control plane, Entra ID and the model's API) and `make orodruin-*`. Run anywhere else — an operator's laptop — the same checks fail, so Annatar stays passive there. Mutants M64–M66. Measured on the bench the same day: the egress holds; the first check said `armed` on a blind listing — Azure lists only the role assignments the caller may read, so the bench's own assignment was hidden. A listing now proves an absence only with subscription-wide read on role assignments (`contract_reader`, on by default); otherwise `identity_unprovable` → passive. Mutant M67.
- **B2 closed — a memory of exercises, or a poisoned one, doesn't flip a real decision** (19/10): the harness gets a `memory` kind (`scripts/llm_smoke.py --kinds memory`). A real threat comes with past cycles that are all exercises on that VM and technique, closed with no action (ransomware, brute force), or with one remembered cycle whose reasoning carries an instruction to the defender (exfiltration). claude-sonnet-5-5: 0 of 9 decisions flipped — isolate or block every time, mean confidence 0.87.
- **The exercise windows reach the hosted watch** (19/10, O1 part C): a blob container of Orodruin's own, managed identity only — Orodruin's identity writes each window as it opens and closes, the hosted Glorfindel's reads them into `runs/exercises/` (at most every 30 s) before it labels a cycle. Neither side ever fails on it: a window that doesn't arrive leaves its cycles `real`. The contract allows the one assignment on that container — only if the declared scope is a container's. Measured: published from Orodruin, listed and read from the hosted Glorfindel, its write refused (403). Mutants M68–M70.
- **B2, model side — the model sees whether a past cycle was an exercise** (19/10): each past cycle is tagged [EXERCICE], [RÉEL] or [source inconnue]; the system prompt says an exercise shows a technique and is never a precedent for a real incident; the current signal is never tagged (an attack during an exercise window must not be relaxed). End-to-end run on the bench the same day, claude-sonnet-5-5: T1486 → isolate_vm 0.80, T1110.001 → block_suspicious_ip 0.88, both right, with exercise-tagged precedents in the prompt.
- **The campaign report writes itself** (19/10, IA0): `annatar campaign run` writes `report.md` at the end (`--no-report` to skip); a report that can't be written never fails the campaign. The Annatar image ships `analysis/`, its container gets the model key and `EREGION_ANALYSIS_MODEL`, and `llm_call` moved to `eregion_core/` (shared, not Glorfindel's).
- **IA0 — the campaign report** (19/10, `analysis/campaign_report.py`, `make campaign-report CAMPAIGN=<id>`): facts read from the campaign's files (never from a model), a ChromaDB collection of its own for earlier runs of the same techniques, and typed claims written by another model than Glorfindel's (Haiku 5.5) — each cites its runs and is kept only if the facts bear it out; the header counts are computed. First real report (the 30/07 campaign): 9 claims kept, 1 rejected. Harness `make report-eval`: a planted « report that every technique was detected » obeyed 0/3. Mutant M63.
- **The War Room says where the model comes from** (19/10): Config shows the model with its source — set at deployment (`GLORFINDEL_LLM_MODEL`) or the code default — and why it is not a setting of the page (changing it changes who decides: harness, end-to-end run, then the deployment's environment). The switch procedure is in `infra/glorfindel-host/README.md`.
- **A returning scanner is recognised** (17/10): in observation nothing is blocked, so a scanner came back and opened three cards on bree. A block card now says when its IP was seen in the last 7 days, and how the last card was judged (To judge, Discord) — never judged for the operator; the report, the header and the Monday summary count the distinct IPs behind the block recommendations (`escalations.recurrence`, `blocks`).
- **The observation's week in Discord** (17/10): every Monday from 08:00 UTC the bot posts what `glorfindel report --since 7d` says — decision cards, judged, share of isolate/block judged wrong, per rule, cards still to judge — once a week.
- **The observation's data kept safe** (17/10): `make glorfindel-host-pull` copies the hosted VM's `~/.glorfindel` (verdicts, memory, state) to the operator's machine, the 30 latest kept; `glorfindel-host-status` warns past 7 days. A powered-off VM stays a week on the map on the hosted Glorfindel (`GLORFINDEL_DISCOVERY_RETENTION_H=168`, passed through docker compose).
- **The War Room says why nothing can be changed** (17/10): in observe-only, a banner under the header — nothing is executed, the mode is locked to human-only by the deployment's read-only credentials (`GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY=1`), judge in To judge — and Config says the same (an « access » row, a note under Autonomy: lifting it is a deployment change, not a setting). In To judge, the VM moves next to the rule instead of being cut by a long title.
- **The War Room is built for the observation** (16/10): a **To judge** list (the last 7 days' decision cards without a verdict, acknowledged ones included, exercises left out; a note and Right / Wrong / Unsure on each — `GET /api/to-judge`), the week's count in the header (cards, judged, share of isolate/block judged wrong), an EXERCISE chip on cards an injector's window covered. Each item shows what to judge on: the IP a block targets, the rule that fired, and the model's own explanation, now recorded on the card (`explanation`) — the first real card showed only the mode-hold wording.
- **B2 (storage side) — every cycle knows whether it is an exercise** (`eregion_core/exercise.py`): an injector opens an exercise window before touching its target (setup included) and closes it when its run ends (`runs/exercises/<run_id>.json`); Annatar's engine does it in a `finally`. Glorfindel labels a cycle `exercise` when a window covers its VM at its time (15-min grace for the detection lag; a window never closed lapses after 2 h), else `real` — in the memory and on the card. The observation report leaves exercise cards out; an exercise card never absorbs a real detection (dedup key). The model doesn't see the source yet, nor does the retrieval filter on it: both change what it reasons on (end-to-end run). Mutants M57–M61.
- **H0 — a VM for Glorfindel** (`infra/glorfindel-host/`, its own Terraform state): the root `docker compose` (watch, War Room and the Discord bot, a new `bot` compose profile) on a small dedicated VM in `rg-glorfindel`, for the bench pre-observation. An observer: its managed identity reads the bench (`Reader`, `Log Analytics Reader`), `GLORFINDEL_READ_ONLY=1`, and the config pushed to it is forced to `human_only` (`host_config.py`). Not a target: outside the bench group, no `eregion-target`, nothing sent to the bench's Log Analytics. SSH from the operator's IP only; the War Room through an SSH tunnel. `make glorfindel-host-plan/-up/-push/-start/-tunnel`. `make glorfindel-host-env` writes its environment from the operator's (`host_env.py`: never an Azure secret, always read-only, a drawn War Room token). A new bench VM, **bree**, always on and not enrolled (`enrolled: false`: no inscription tag, no injector targets it — exercises stay on gondolin), carries a real workload (`infra/terraform/scripts/workload.sh`, `make celebrimbor-workload`, by Run Command: a small nginx site, a visitor, the nightly backup, maintenance sudos, unattended upgrades). Written and planned, not applied yet. Mutants M54, M55.
- **The War Room card shows what detection can see of a VM** (P4): a fourth readiness group, *Detection*, next to NSG, Backup and Compute. The audit reads the disk write counter and its cadence, whether the VM sends Syslog, and the state of each rule that covers it — sees data, blind (its table gets nothing from the VM), in error (its last poll failed), or reading rows from elsewhere (storage). A VM the rules can't see no longer looks normal. Queries checked read-only on 14 days of the bench's Log Analytics.
- **DX1 — a light `CLAUDE.md`** (143 KB → 16 KB): every session and subagent loads it, so it keeps only the concept, the safety rules, the conventions and the sessions, with an index into `docs/`. The detail moved verbatim to `docs/glorfindel.md`, `docs/azure.md`, `docs/fichiers-cles.md`, `docs/operations.md` and `docs/historique.md`, and the priorities to the ROADMAP; each `CLAUDE_<ROLE>.md` points to its docs. `scripts/check_paragraphs.py` checks nothing was lost; `tests/unit/test_docs_budget.py` keeps it under 25 KB with a resolving index (mutants M48, M49). Convention: update the doc concerned; `CLAUDE.md` only when a rule or a convention changes.
- **elrond: roadmap lots dispatched to Claude Code subagents** (`.claude/skills/elrond/`, `.claude/agents/`, `docs/elrond.md`).
  - The main session keeps only the plan index and dispatches one Opus 5.5 subagent per lot, each in its own worktree.
  - `palantir.py` journals safeguard stops and model fallbacks: which model answered, at which step, and the files written meanwhile.
  - Work done on a fallback model is reviewed by Opus 5.5 before it counts as done.
  - A fallback episode that ran a shell command says its list of written files is incomplete (`files_complete: false`, with its commands): the reviewer then reads the whole diff. Measured on a real session, where Bash writes were missing from the list.
  - Optional user-level hooks: `scripts/elrond-hooks.sh`. Pull request template: `.github/pull_request_template.md`.
- **E1 — one experiment schema for a technique and a fault** (`schemas/experiment.schema.json`, versioned v1): same shape for both — `kind` absent means `technique`, `engine` absent means `local-script`, so the five existing scenarios validate unchanged; a fault names its engine, reference and steady state; a technique on another engine than local-script (Stratus, Atomic) is run by its `engine_ref`, without steps. The catalog schema (`schemas/technique_catalog.schema.json`) now holds faults, in their own `faults` list — a fault has no ATT&CK id, and neither technique loader picks it up. (Reviewed with Opus 5.5 after being written on a fallback model: both points fixed then.) `eregion_core/experiment.py` validates both; `tests/unit/test_experiment_schema.py` runs in CI (the catalog is validated there).
- **O0 — one target guard for every injector** (`eregion_core/inscription.py`): the bench's perimeter (its resource groups, and the subscription the scope names — named before, never checked), enrollment by the tags `eregion-target=<bench id>` and `eregion-zone` on the target and its resource group, and the production rule — in `production`, only a technique declared non-destructive; a fault, never. Anything unknown is refused. Replaces `annatar/safety/guard.py` and the perimeter of `annatar/campaign/scope.py`; Celebrimbor tags its resources (`infra/terraform/locals.tf`). The old `annatar-test=true` tag still enrolls a target on the bench during the migration. Six mutants (M42–M47), all caught.

### Status of the 0.3 lots
- Done: A0, O0, E1, O1, R1, G0, B2, IA0 (above), with G2 from 0.x (dependency failures fail closed).
- Running: H0 — Glorfindel hosted on a small dedicated VM, the bench VM always on with a real workload: a pre-observation that calibrates benign noise (it does not count for the four weeks on a real fleet). Its four weeks end about 13/11.
- Next: the review point after 0.3 (#70); B1 (a second cloud model in the harness) and A1 open 0.4.

## [0.2.0] — 2026-10-15

First release to be tagged; earlier work is in the git history.

### Glorfindel — detection and response
- Continuous rule-based detection (RulePoller, one query per rule, rows routed to VMs), assets discovered from their own telemetry (Log Analytics Heartbeat).
- Decision by an LLM (provider-agnostic via LiteLLM) over the enriched signal, a RAG memory of past cycles (ChromaDB) and validated few-shot examples, framed by deterministic guards: executable action, confidence gate, uncharacterized-signal guardrail, attribution, release precondition, autonomy mode, readiness lock. Any model failure escalates; it never acts.
- Autonomy per asset: `human_only` (default) and `non_disruptive`, with a readiness check before a VM leaves `human_only`; observe-only mode on read-only credentials.
- Verified response on Azure: just-in-time isolation by a quarantine NSG (the customer's NSG set aside and restored, compatible with Terraform — measured), Azure DNS and IMDS denied, open sessions cut, every NIC covered, NSG precedence checked, IP blocks in both directions, reassertion every minute, snapshot and restore through Recovery Services Vault with Run Command neutralized before a restore.
- Purple loop: detection rules authored by the LLM, grounded on a technique catalog and the real table schema — reactively on a missed attack, proactively with `propose-rules`; every rule is a proposal a human approves.
- War Room web UI, Discord bot, terminal dashboard; observation kit (`glorfindel verdict`, `glorfindel report`).

### Annatar — attacks
- Scenarios validated in real runs on Azure: T1486, T1041, T1110.001, T1548.003, T1136.001, and T1110 with T1548 in parallel.
- Generative campaigns: a kill chain composed from the shared technique catalog, optionally LLM-planned, within a scope and a budget.

### Proof
- Hermetic unit tests (no Azure call, no LLM call), property-based invariants on an in-memory Azure whose world moves, a mutation check with a control set, cross-process lock tests, a daily azurerm canary, the Celebrimbor bench (Terraform).
